### 5.9 Portal Builder package (planned, both editions)

Owner decision (Brandon, 2026-10-25; D-118): BarnCRM Portal Builder is an optional module, offered in either edition, that builds logged-in portals from templates. Portal Builder itself depends on Core only; each template needs only its own module and is offered only when that module is installed. Access direction (Brandon, 2026-10-25): "For the board portal, I'd like a solution similar to Llama Sites or Titan portals that don't require Salesforce licenses for logins." Requirement (Brandon, 2026-10-26; D-120): "There needs to be at least one option to build with Salesforce with logins but without additional cost." Portal users log in at no additional Salesforce license cost: the on-platform Salesforce Site option with BarnCRM's own login layer (Section 11.2 item 30, option B) is required, the off-platform option (A) becomes an optional later connector, and confirming (B) as the build path waits on a check of Salesforce's contract terms (Section 11.2 item 30). The ADR before build must address D-09, ADR-0013 and the Section 1.5 non-goal on portals. Portal Builder supersedes Section 6.1.1 item 12 (Website and portal tool) and pairs with the Forms module (C-42). Planned after the Core quality pass and after C-45 and C-46, not scheduled to an iteration; a new ADR is needed before build. Documents served are Salesforce Files and count against the org's file storage.

### 5.10 LWC Builder package (planned, both editions)

Owner request (Brandon, 2026-10-26; D-120): "I'd also like to add an LWC builder as a module. I'd like it to have a drag and drop grid builder based interface so people can create their own LWCs declaratively." BarnCRM LWC Builder is an optional module, offered in either edition, depending on Core only. Planned after v1.0, not scheduled to an iteration; a new ADR is needed before build. Admins build their own components on a drag-and-drop grid canvas, preview them, then place them on Lightning pages.

Design note (recommendation for the ADR): one packaged runtime renderer LWC, placed in Lightning App Builder like any other component, renders a saved component definition (JSON stored in a BarnCRM record or in custom metadata; the ADR decides which). Nothing is deployed at run time, so no Metadata API callout, Named Credential or deploy permission is needed, and a definition is ordinary data that can be versioned and moved between orgs. The renderer reads in user mode, so every built component honours the viewing user's object and field permissions and sharing. Alternative recorded, not recommended for v1: generating real LWC source bundles and deploying them through the Metadata API to the org's own API, which needs a callout setup, deploy permissions and, for an unlocked package, the "Deploy Metadata from Non-Certified Package Versions via Apex" setting (the CMDT Manager constraint, Section 6.1 item 9). Exporting a definition as LWC source for developers comes later (LB-06). Research: Section 6.1.2 item 19.

| ID | Feature | Size | Pri | Iter | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| LB-01 | Grid canvas builder: drag-and-drop layout of rows, columns and sections, preview, and save as a named component definition | M | P2 | 1.x | Admin only; a definition is data, not deployed metadata |
| LB-02 | Runtime renderer: one packaged LWC placed in Lightning App Builder (record, app and home pages) that renders a chosen definition | M | P2 | 1.x | User-mode reads; honours CRUD, FLS and sharing; no Metadata API callout or Named Credential |
| LB-03 | Building blocks: the page record's fields, related lists, record lists, charts from standard reports, text and images | M | P2 | 1.x | Charts from standard reports only (no CRM Analytics) |
| LB-04 | Buttons that launch a quick action or a flow | m | P2 | 1.x | Runs only what the user may run |
| LB-05 | Conditional visibility per block by field value or by permission | m | P2 | 1.x | A display rule, never a security control; record access still comes from sharing |
| LB-06 | Export a definition as LWC source for developers | m | P2 | 1.x | Later than LB-01 to LB-05; the exported bundle is the developer's to maintain |

### 5.11 Scholarships and Outbound Grants package (planned, Nonprofit Edition proposed)

Owner request (Brandon, 2026-10-26; D-122): "Please do the same for Scholarships/Outbound Grants." BarnCRM Scholarships and Outbound Grants is an optional module for organizations that give money: scholarships, fellowships, mini-grants and other outbound grants. It reverses the non-goal "Grantmaking as a funder" (Section 1.5, Section 11.3). It depends on Core only; Giving and Funders are optional links. Edition: Nonprofit Edition (proposed). Planned after Core QA and the current module round (D-38), release target TBD, not v1.0; a new ADR and canonical model update before build. Coexistence: orgs on NPC Grantmaking or Outbound Funds are detected only (Section 6.1.4); migration from Outbound Funds is a later decision (Section 11.2 item 32, O-6).

### 5.12 Event Management package (planned, both editions proposed)

Owner request (Brandon, 2026-10-26; D-123): "I think I'd also like it to have an event management module (similar to Summit Event App but easier to use and more full feature, including the ability to manage a Dreamin Conference with tracks, sessions, speakers, speaking applications, a portal for speakers, integration to volunteering, sponsorship management, etc.)." BarnCRM Event Management replaces the post-1.0 Events package (Section 6.1 item 2) and keeps Summit Events compatibility (Section 6.1.1 item 3). It depends on Core only; Volunteers, Giving, Funders and Portal Builder are optional links. Edition: both editions (proposed). Planned after Core QA and the current module round (D-38), release target TBD, not v1.0; a new ADR and canonical model update before build. Constraints (proposed): no Campaign dependency (standard-object rule; Campaign sync, if wanted, belongs in Mirror); public registration pages need a guest-user security design (as V-09 and C-42); BarnCRM takes no payments (Section 1.5): ticket and sponsorship payments are made through the org's own processor and recorded through Mirror's inbound gift API (X-03) when Giving is installed.

| ID | Feature | Size | Pri | Iter | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| EV-01 | Events: single, multi-day or recurring, venue or virtual, capacity, status, public event page | M | P2 | TBD | Guest-user design needed |
| EV-02 | Registrations and attendees: registrants matched to Core people without duplicates (household-aware), waitlist, check-in, attendance | M | P2 | TBD | |
| EV-03 | Ticket types: price, quantity, sales dates, promo codes | m | P2 | TBD | Payments outside BarnCRM (constraint above) |
| EV-04 | Tracks, sessions and agenda: tracks, rooms, time slots, session capacity and sign-up, published agenda | M | P2 | TBD | |
| EV-05 | Speakers: profiles on Core people, session assignments, needs (travel, A/V) | m | P2 | TBD | |
| EV-06 | Call for speakers: speaker applications with review, scoring, and accept or decline notices | M | P2 | TBD | Same review pattern as SG-03, no dependency on it |
| EV-07 | Speaker portal through Portal Builder (PB-07) | M | P2 | TBD | Needs Portal Builder |
| EV-08 | Volunteer integration: event volunteer jobs and shifts in Volunteers | m | P2 | TBD | Volunteers optional, through an optional link |
| EV-09 | Sponsorship management: sponsor organizations, tiers, benefits, deliverables checklist (logo, booth, passes), status | M | P2 | TBD | Sponsorship linked to a Giving gift or commitment, or a Funders award, when installed (optional links) |
| EV-10 | Summit Events compatibility: Summit registrants land in BarnCRM people and households without duplicates | m | P2 | TBD | Lead matching only in Mirror or a small adapter (standard-object rule) |

## 6. Roadmap by iteration

Each iteration targets about three major and five minor features, plus engineering work. Iterations are not time-boxed to a calendar; they are done when the definition of done (Section 7.4) is met. Realistic pace for one architect with AI agents: two to four weeks per iteration. Do not start an iteration's features until the previous iteration's package version is promoted and installed in the test orgs.

| Iteration | Theme | Major | Minor | Engineering |
|---|---|---|---|---|

| **v0.2** | Giving core | G-01, C-13, C-14 | G-02, G-03, G-04, G-05, G-06 (plus C-12 Setup Assistant full) | Rollup build-vs-vendor decision recorded (later superseded by D-16, 2026-10-12); performance baseline (10k gifts) |
| **v0.3** | Commitments, relationships, addresses | G-07, C-15, C-17 | C-16, G-08, G-09, G-10, G-11 | Second package (Giving) published |
| **v0.4** | Stewardship and receipting | G-12, G-13, G-14 | G-15, G-16, G-18, C-18, gift receipt PDF branding settings | PDF generation approach settled (Visualforce render as PDF vs LWC print) |
| **v0.5** | Import 2.0, gift import, and coexistence hardening | C-19, G-17, C-22, G-23 gift import | C-20, C-21, G-24 donation matching, NPC and NPSP import templates, XLSX support, undo retention setting | Person Account scratch org shape as a locally gated org: the long-lived `oi-pa` scratch org with Person Accounts (owner decision, 2026-09-24), tested through `scripts/org/run-org-tests.sh`, not a GitHub CI matrix. Four major features, above the usual three, because gift import does not work today (no package implements `ImportEntityProcessor`; the Core class looks up `GiftImportProcessor`, which does not exist) and the first customers must migrate gifts (owner decision, 2026-09-23) |

Duplicate rule matching for any object (#405) and the rest of the inventory landed in #401 to #408 (cleanups, Access add-on permission sets, merge settlement, coverage floor, import undo, Platform-license permissions, empty household tidy). ### 6.0a Release plan (owner decision, 2026-10-31, D-155)

Owner request (Brandon, 2026-10-31): "Please make sure anything open on the roadmap is broken up by releases (~3 major enhancements and ~3-6 minor enhancements per release). Please make sure this release management, tracking, and updates happen automatically (including updates if I ask for specific features to be moved up or moved back) going forward. I'd like batches to include 2-3 releases."

This subsection is the schedule for every open, approved item. Where Sections 6.0, 6.1 and the iteration table above say "not scheduled", "release target TBD" or name an old iteration, this subsection governs. Rules:

- Each release holds about 3 major enhancements (a new feature, tool or module slice of substantial size) and 3 to 6 minor enhancements (follow-ups, small features, polish). Sizes follow the feature catalog (Section 5), except that portal templates built on the shipped Portal Builder (PB-03 to PB-08) count as minor here.
- Brandon's standing authorization (2026-10-31, D-156): new features consistent with the vision may be added to releases without his approval; new modules or editions, reversals of settled decisions or non-goals, and not-approved items go to him as proposals (Section 6.1.5).
- Order: Brandon's explicit sequencing first; Core before modules (D-38, never dropping functionality); dependencies (C-45 and C-46 before Corporate Hub and Portal Builder, D-118; a new ADR before each new module); then impact.
- Each landing batch covers 2 or 3 releases. Feature PRs and the incremental gate run per landing branch as today (D-141, D-142); the release gate, the `barn-npc` and `mwdbdevsb` deploys (Brandon's approval each time) and QA and UX testing run once per batch. Each release ships its own What's New record (`Release_Note`, checked by `check-whats-new.py --release`; 0.11 landed in #702).
- Release numbers continue from 0.11, the last shipped version. The old iterations v0.12 (Beta) and v0.13 (AppExchange) are no longer releases: they become milestones (below), because they wait on outside events, not on build work. Release 0.17 completes the v1.0 scope (the v0.9 and v0.10 iterations, plus the Core, Giving, Volunteers and Programs additions of D-156); it stays the v1.0 candidate. v1.0 is tagged once Brandon's initial QA is complete, expected around or after release 0.17 and before AppExchange approval (milestone M-1, D-157). Releases after it are numbered 1.1, 1.2 and so on.
- Each batch starts testing only after the whole-solution review (Section 9.8); new work follows the portability preferences toward BarnCRM for WordPress (Section 9.9) where they cost little.
- Statuses: Planned, In progress, Landed (in `main` through a landing batch), Shipped (the batch's release gate, deploys and QA are done).
- Upkeep is automatic (Section 9.7): every newly approved item is placed in a release; a move up or back that Brandon asks for is re-slotted and the affected releases rebalanced; statuses change as batches land.

#### Not approved (not in any release)

Section 6.1.1 item 6 (document builder); BarnCRM for WordPress as an edition (portability is a design preference meanwhile, Section 9.9); community processor webhooks beyond the Stripe receiver (X-08); donor scoring maturity beyond G-19, G-26, G-29 and C-64; multi-currency; localization. Each joins a release only when Brandon approves it. The eleven Section 6.1.5 proposals were approved 2026-10-31 (D-157) and are placed above.

### 6.1 Post-1.0 themes (parking, ordered by expected demand)

1. Memberships (G-22) as its own package.
2. Events package (registration, attendance, simple ticketing without payments). **Replaced 2026-10-26 (Brandon, D-123)** by the Event Management module (Section 5.12, EV-01 to EV-11), planned after Core QA and the current module round, release target TBD.
3. Public volunteer sign-up (V-09) after a guest-user security design. Related: Portal Builder's Volunteer portal template (PB-03, D-118) covers logged-in volunteers.
4. Community-contributed processor webhooks (X-08).
5. Donor scoring maturity, interaction summaries, major-gift portfolios.
6. Multi-currency.
7. AI-readiness pack: documented export schemas and a canonical-model JSON export so any AI tool can be grounded in clean data. Later, after the C-51 and C-52 batch (Brandon, 2026-10-30, D-154).
8. Localization (Spanish first).
9. CMDT Manager (for consideration, not scheduled; Brandon, 2026-09-22): an in-app tab, in both editions, where holders of a CMDT Admin permission set add, edit and archive custom metadata records through a familiar list view and record view. Known constraints: saving a record is an asynchronous metadata deployment; an unlocked package needs the org setting "Deploy Metadata from Non-Certified Package Versions via Apex"; the editor needs Customize Application and Modify Metadata, which is why the permission set is an admin one; standard list views and record pages do not support custom metadata, so the UI is custom; the Apex Metadata API cannot delete records, so archive is a field on each editable type. Moving user-editable configuration to custom objects was offered as the simpler alternative and is not accepted yet. **Built, landing (PRs #217, #218)** (ADR-0085, D-25): a section of BarnCRM Settings (General), not a tab; only the org's own relationship types are editable, shipped types are read only; the BarnCRM Metadata Admin permission set carries no Customize Application or Modify Metadata, and it alone grants `Manage_BarnCRM_Metadata`.
10. Analytics module, with field utilization as its first feature (for consideration, not scheduled; Brandon, 2026-09-23). For each object, and each record type where one is used, the share of records that hold a value in each field, with trends from saved snapshots, so an administrator can see which fields nobody fills in, which are worth making required, and which can be retired. A batch job counts field values in the org and stores each run as snapshot records, reported through standard reports and dashboards, with no CRM Analytics dependency. It is neutral, so both suites get it. It works with the data hygiene console (C-28) and the data management tools: a low-use field can go straight into a query or a bulk update. "Where is this field used" (page layouts, record pages, reports, flows) needs the Tooling or Metadata API, which means a callout to the org's own API; that part waits for the same decision as connections to other systems. **Built, landing (PRs #217, #218)** (ADR-0086, D-26): it ships in Core, not as its own package; a weekly user-mode count (Sundays 04:00) per object and record type, kept 13 months as `Field_Utilization_Snapshot__c`, on BarnCRM Settings > Health; Account, Contact and custom objects only (other standard objects would belong in Mirror). **Snapshot History (Brandon, 2026-10-26, D-133):** the Field Utilization page's button is "Save Utilization Snapshot" (hint: "Keeps this count so your next count shows what changed."), and a Snapshot History list below the results shows the saved counts, with compare, view and delete, newest 50 per object.
11. Data management 2 (C-36 typed SOQL, subqueries, aggregates, XLSX export, inline edit; C-37 transforms, dedupe on import, bulk delete). Moved into v0.7 on 2026-10-12: C-36 and C-37 (parts 1 and 2, including bulk delete) are built and in `main` (#219).
12. Scheduled imports and connections to other systems (C-38), owner decision 2026-09-23.
13. BarnCRM Territory Management (C-40), an optional module for both editions depending on Core only (planned as a Core Super Tool by D-98; made a module, Brandon, 2026-10-26, D-120): assign records (people, households, organizations) to territories and owners by rules (for example geography, postal code, type or size), with a preview before reassigning and undo. Planned, after v1.0, not scheduled (Brandon, 2026-10-14; D-98, D-120). A new ADR before build.
14. Grid tool (C-41), a Core Super Tool: a spreadsheet-style editable grid to view and edit many related records on one screen (inline edit, related records in one view, saved grids), comparable to paid marketplace grid add-ons. Planned, after v1.0 (Brandon, 2026-10-14; D-98).
15. Forms module (C-42): a form builder that creates and updates BarnCRM records (people, households, organizations, gifts, volunteer sign-ups) with conditional logic, multi-step forms, spam protection, and matching against existing records to avoid duplicates. Supersedes the "Forms and surveys" idea (#16 in 6.1.1) and builds on V-09's public-page approach. Added to the plan by owner request, 2026-10-17; not yet scheduled. Needs a decision log entry and a guest-user security design before build.
16. BarnCRM for WordPress (future edition, not scheduled): a WordPress edition of BarnCRM built on Brandon's WordPress platform plugin (content builder, listings, filters, form builder, automation builder; see [WordPress Platform Plugin](/specification/wordpress-platform-plugin.md)). The canonical data model (4.4) is the shared layer between the Salesforce and WordPress editions. Owner request, 2026-10-17.
17. Multi-brand management (C-47), a Core feature for both editions: one organization running several brands (for example a parent nonprofit with programs, chapters, affiliates, a thrift store or DBAs, each with its own name, logo and contact details). Brand records (name, logo, address, phone, website, colors, default email sender as an org-wide email address, letter signature, active flag; one default brand), each belonging to a Legal Entity record (below); brands assigned to people, households and organizations many-to-many through a junction (one person can relate to several brands and legal entities), and a Brand lookup in modules on gifts, appeals, campaigns, volunteer jobs and programs, defaulting from the creating user's or the parent record's brand, set in bulk through Bulk Update and mapped on import; brand-aware outputs (Giving receipts and acknowledgments, Volunteers hour letters, Corporate Hub documents, Portal Builder portals use the record's brand instead of the single org branding; receipt numbering per brand optional); brand-scoped work (list views, reports and dashboards filterable by brand; optional brand-restricted visibility, below; staff can belong to several brands). Standard Salesforce features only (no Experience Cloud), canonical model updated before any object is added, namespace-neutral, and compatible with Nonprofit Cloud coexistence (Mirror) without Core touching NPC objects. Module features consume it. Planned, after v1.0, not scheduled (Brandon, 2026-10-26; D-119, D-120).
    - **Legal entities (D-120).** Owner direction (Brandon, 2026-10-26): "For multi-brand management, some of these may be different legal entities so please consider that for impact." Recommended model: a Legal Entity record that one or more brands belong to (each brand points to one; the default entity is the org itself), rather than an "Is legal entity" flag on Brand, so brands of one entity share its legal details. Each legal entity holds its legal name, tax or registration ID, registered address and fiscal year start. Impact: gifts, funds, receipts and receipt numbering belong to the receiving legal entity (Giving); accounting and gift exports filter by legal entity; Corporate Hub compliance obligations and templates (annual report, tax return, GDPR controller) are per legal entity; under GDPR the data controller is the legal entity, so consent and communication preferences may differ per brand or entity; moving a gift between entities is a correction with an audit trail, never a silent edit.
    - **Brand-restricted visibility (optional, off by default; D-120, resolves Section 11.2 item 31).** When on, a user sees a record only when they hold access to at least one of the record's brands; records with no brand follow the org's normal sharing, and admins with View All bypass it. Each brand gets a public group; users are given brands on the Access page; the org-wide default for the affected objects is Private; BarnCRM keeps share rows in step when record brands or user brands change (Apex managed sharing with a BarnCRM sharing reason on custom objects; AccountShare and related rows for Account and Contact), with a nightly reconcile and a Health Check. Standard sharing rules cannot express many-to-many brand membership, which is why custom share rows are needed: this is custom sharing code, a departure from standard sharing only for this feature, and it needs an ADR before build. Large orgs: share-row volume, recalculation time and data skew (many records under one owner or one brand group) must be measured before release.
18. BarnCRM LWC Builder (LB-01 to LB-06, Section 5.10), an optional module for both editions depending on Core only: admins build their own Lightning components declaratively on a drag-and-drop grid canvas, preview them and place them on Lightning pages, shown by one packaged runtime renderer that reads saved definitions in user mode. Planned, after v1.0, not scheduled (Brandon, 2026-10-26; D-120). A new ADR before build.
19. Funders Grant Finder and full grant management (F-09 to F-13, Section 5.5; D-121), Nonprofit Edition: Grants.gov search (free public API), application tasks and checklists, LOI, application and report documents, budget versus awarded, renewals. Planned after Core QA and the current module round, release target TBD.
20. BarnCRM Scholarships and Outbound Grants (SG-01 to SG-08, Section 5.11; D-122), Nonprofit Edition proposed: grantmaking programs and cycles, applications, reviewers and scoring, awards, disbursements, recipient reporting, applicant portal. Planned after Core QA and the current module round, release target TBD. A new ADR before build.
21. Integrations and migrations (D-125): the ten opportunities in Section 6.1.4, after Core QA and development, preferably after v1.0.

#### 6.1.1 Proposed Super Tools and features (Brandon to decide, recorded 2026-10-14)

None of these is approved or scheduled except C-40 (D-98; a module since D-120), C-41 (D-98), C-47 (D-119, D-120) and the LWC Builder module (D-120), all planned after v1.0 and listed here for ranking only; and, approved 2026-10-26, item 1 (DLRS parity, D-124, being built now) and item 3 (Event Management, D-123, release target TBD). Approach for community tools: compatibility with existing Salesforce Open Source Commons solutions first, BarnCRM's own version later only if wanted (Brandon, 2026-10-14). Ranked best fit first. Comparable tools are researched in 6.1.2.

| # | Idea | Edition | Approach | Comparable tools |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | DLRS compatibility | Both | Confirm DLRS rollups work on BarnCRM objects alongside BarnCRM's rollups and document it; later the Rollup Builder imports DLRS rollups. Rollup Builder is an optional module (D-101, landed #438), so DLRS-only orgs need not install it. **Approved 2026-10-26 (Brandon, D-124), being built now:** DLRS parity or better, detection and same-field guard, DLRS import wizard, Rollup Helper detect plus guide (Section 4.10) | DLRS, Rollup Helper, Easy Rollup, NPSP Customizable Rollups |
| 2 | Grid tool (C-41, planned after v1.0) | Both | Spreadsheet-style editing of many related records, saved grids; builds on C-36 inline edit of Find results | GridBuddy Connect (Validity), Conga Grid, Grids (RavenApps), CMTD Enhanced Related List (Salesforce Labs), native list view inline edit |
| 3 | Summit Events compatibility, then BarnCRM Event Management (Section 5.12, D-123) | Both | Registrants land in BarnCRM correctly (households, no duplicates); Lead matching only in Mirror or a small adapter (standard-object rule). **Approved 2026-10-26 (Brandon, D-123):** BarnCRM's own Event Management module (EV-01 to EV-11), easier and fuller than Summit Events, able to run a community "Dreamin" conference; Summit compatibility kept (EV-10) | Summit Events App, Blackthorn Events, Fonteva Events, Cvent, Click and Pledge, Eventbrite |
| 4 | Advanced duplicate management (**moved to C-51, 2026-10-30, D-154**) | Both | Builds on Duplicate Review: whole-org scheduled scans, fuzzy and household-aware matching, merge rules for which value wins, auto-merge of exact matches, blocking on import | Plauti Deduplicate (was Duplicate Check), Validity DemandTools and DupeBlocker, Cloudingo, ZoomInfo Operations (was RingLead), DataGroomr, Insycle |
| 5 | Memberships Essentials compatibility, BarnCRM membership later | Both (dues as gifts would be a Giving extra) | Compatibility with the Commons membership project first; a Core membership feature later if wanted (G-22 today is Giving only) | Memberships Essentials (Commons), Fonteva, Nimble AMS |
| 6 | Document builder | Both | Letters, PDFs and mail merge from templates, generalizing the Giving receipts engine | Conga Composer, S-Docs, Nintex DocGen (was Drawloop), Docomotion, Formstack Documents (was WebMerge), Ascendix Composer, DocuSign Gen |
| 7 | Access explorer (**next batch, 2026-10-30, D-154**) | Both | Who can see and edit what, and why; extends the Access page and Health Check | Salesforce User Access Summary, User Access Policies, Security Center, Elements.cloud Permissions Explorer |
| 8 | BarnCRM Territory Management (C-40, optional module, planned after v1.0, D-120) | Both | Rule-based assignment of records to territories and owners, with preview and undo; a separate package depending on Core | Salesforce Enterprise Territory Management, assignment rules, Distribution Engine, LeanData, Chili Piper, Kubaru |

#### 6.1.2 Comparable tools research (2026-10-14)

Method: web search plus a few page fetches; AppExchange listings were not opened. Prices are indicative only (vendor blogs, review roundups; many are quote-based). Rows marked UNVERIFIED come from background knowledge or one weak source; confirm on the AppExchange listing or vendor page before relying on them. Corrections found: Own was acquired by Salesforce (closed 2024-11-18), its products now Salesforce Backup and Recover and Salesforce Archive; Plauti Duplicate Check is now Plauti Deduplicate; RingLead is part of ZoomInfo (2021); Pardot is Marketing Cloud Account Engagement; GridBuddy is now GridBuddy Connect; Salesforce Optimizer reported retired in favor of Org Check (one source); "Llama Sites" not found. Grid prices come from a SalesforceBen article whose author founded RavenApps.

#### 6.1.3 Considered and not recommended (recorded 2026-10-14)

Reviewed with the 6.1.1 list and not recommended for BarnCRM; kept for reference so they are not re-proposed without a new reason.

| Idea | Why not | Comparable tools |
|---|---|---|
| Quoting, pricing and configuration (CPQ) | Sales-pipeline tooling outside BarnCRM's people and households focus; needs Opportunity and Product objects, which Core may not use (standard-object rule) | Salesforce CPQ (Revenue Cloud), Conga CPQ, DealHub |
| E-signature | Legal and compliance weight, paid signing services; better integrated than rebuilt | DocuSign, Adobe Acrobat Sign, Conga Sign |
| Marketing automation and mass email | Salesforce mass email limits, deliverability and consent obligations; a specialist tool does it better | Marketing Cloud Account Engagement, Mailchimp connectors |
| Phone systems (CTI) | Needs a paid telephony provider and per-user setup | Salesforce Voice, Dialpad, RingCentral |
| Payment processing in Core | Belongs in Mirror (planned processor webhooks, X-08) and the processors themselves | Stripe, Click and Pledge, iATS |
| AI features that call a paid service | Conflicts with the no paid AI API rule; the AI-readiness pack (post-1.0) covers grounding other tools instead | Agentforce, Einstein |

#### 6.1.4 Integration and migration opportunities (review 2026-10-26)

Source: the integration and migration market review of 2026-10-26 (read-only research; V4S, PMM, Party Relationship Groups and popular nonprofit tools). Owner rules (Brandon, 2026-10-26, D-125): integrations wait until Core QA and development are done, preferably after v1.0 unless applicable now; the in-app Integrations page (C-48, being built now) is roadmap only ("In Progress") with a request path for users and providers; Mass Action Scheduler is never added or integrated (unmaintained); tools that overlap a BarnCRM module (V4S, PMM, Outbound Funds) are mentioned only when installed in the client org; complementary apps (Summit Events, Community Commons Membership, DLRS) may be listed always. Key finding: most migration sources (NPSP, V4S, PMM, Outbound Funds, Nonprofit Cloud) sit in the same org, so an in-org source for the Import engine is the main enabler; migrations that read Opportunity, Campaign, Gift Transaction or Party Relationship Group live in Mirror or a migration package (standard-object rule). Decisions are open (Section 11.2 item 32).

Post-1.0 roadmap items, ranked:

1. Migration Assistant framework: an in-org source for Import, ordered multi-step runs, verification, retire checklist.
2. Nonprofit Cloud Party Relationship Groups to BarnCRM households, plus NPC relationships and affiliations (Section 11.2 item 9).
3. V4S to BarnCRM Volunteers, with public volunteer sign-up (V-09).
4. Nonprofit Cloud gifts (Gift Transaction, Commitment, Designation, Soft Credit) to Giving, in-org, beside X-07.
5. NPSP migration kit: adopt households in place (X-05), then relationships, affiliations, addresses, recurring donations, allocations, soft credits; Opportunity backfill through X-01.
6. Online giving landing: a first-party Stripe receiver (X-08) and Zapier recipes, plus a compatibility note for tools that write NPSP Opportunities through Mirror.
7. Off-Salesforce import templates: DonorPerfect, Bloomerang, Little Green Light (then Neon, CiviCRM, Raiser's Edge NXT).
8. Rollup migration: DLRS and NPSP customizable rollups into Rollup Builder (the DLRS import wizard is approved now, D-124).
9. PMM to BarnCRM Programs (check P-01 is PMM-mappable now; migration after v0.9).
10. Integrations page in BarnCRM Settings (C-48, being built now, roadmap only), later with detected apps, compatibility notes and migration links.

#### 6.1.5 Competitive research (2026-10-31, D-156)

Owner request (Brandon, 2026-10-31): "Please review all the tools and capabilities I've mentioned before and research all the capabilities/features in those tools and compare them to what's available or planned for BarnCRM. Please use the results of this to get ideas for future enhancements and add those to the roadmap. Please prioritize them as you see fit by their impact/effort. Impact can be weighted higher than just the ratio. These new features and enhancements can be added to the roadmap and releases without my approval as long as they're consistent with the type of solution I'm looking to build."

Method: web research on 2026-10-31 across the tools named in this plan and BMemory, plus a few comparators. Most vendor facts come from review and aggregator pages, Trailhead and project documentation; they are UNVERIFIED for external use. Specifically UNVERIFIED: Neon, Raiser's Edge NXT and Classy (background knowledge only); Nimble AMS and Fonteva chapter and certification detail; V4S, PMM, Outbound Funds and Summit Events were read from Trailhead and project docs, not current package versions; Galaxy Digital and "Field Footprint" were not found. Pricing is omitted (sources disagree).

**Tools covered.** Suites and donor platforms: NPSP, Nonprofit Cloud (Agentforce Nonprofit), CiviCRM, Bloomerang, DonorPerfect, Little Green Light, Neon, Raiser's Edge NXT, Classy. Admin and data tools: Jetstream, DLRS, Salesforce Inspector Reloaded, Data Loader and Data Import Wizard, dataloader.io, Workbench, DemandTools, DupeBlocker, Cloudingo, Plauti Deduplicate, DataGroomr, Insycle, Field Trip, Org Check, Elements.cloud, Sonar, AbstraLinx, org-analyzer, Arovy, document builders (Conga Composer, S-Docs, Nintex DocGen, Docomotion, Formstack Documents, DocuSign Gen), map tools, backup, archiving and field history tools (Mass Action Scheduler recorded only, D-125). Modules: V4S, PMM, Outbound Funds, Summit Events, Memberships Essentials, Fonteva, Nimble AMS, Blackthorn Events, Cvent, Eventbrite, Click and Pledge, Nonprofit Cloud Grantmaking and Program Management, Experience Cloud and Sites, Titan, LlamaSite, FormAssembly, Formstack, territory and routing tools, Bloomerang Volunteer, VolunteerHub, Submittable, Fluxx, Foundant, BoardEffect, OnBoard, Boardable.

**Gaps by area.**
- Fundraising: BarnCRM matches or beats NPSP on households, gift entry, commitments, soft credits, levels, stewardship, receipts and retention reports. Gaps: an Opportunity-free solicitation pipeline, ask cadence, saved segments for outside email tools, pledge reminders, recurring donor health (needs processor data), DAF and wealth fields. Online giving pages, peer-to-peer, mass email and wealth screening stay outside BarnCRM.
- Admin and data: Find, Super Importer, Super Transform, duplicates, Data Dictionary and Org Optimizer cover most of Jetstream, Data Loader and DemandTools for small orgs. Gaps: who changed Setup, an admin digest, a record field inspector, permission compare, where-used before retiring a field, automation and technical debt in the Data Dictionary, merge beyond Account and Contact. Backup, archiving, field history and radius search are proposals (Section 6.1.1 items not yet approved).
- Programs: thinnest against PMM and Nonprofit Cloud: cohorts and sessions, service plans and goals, referrals, eligibility screening, aggregate counts.
- Volunteers: strong on staff-side management; gaps are reminders, matching, waitlists, groups, minors, recognition and the value of hours. Self-service sign-up (V-09) is the largest gap and needs Brandon.
- Funders and grantmaking: answer library; conditional disbursements and reviewer panels for Scholarships and Outbound Grants.
- Events: QR check-in, gala seating, reminders and no-shows, appointments.
- Board and governance: skills matrix, action items, committees, packet read tracking, self-assessment, availability poll.
- Memberships: the largest structural gap (only G-22 and a compatibility note); a module needs Brandon.

**Considered, not added.** Search Everywhere (standard global search covers it); data model diagram (low value); scheduled saved mass actions (Mass Action Scheduler pattern, D-125).

**Out of scope (conflicts with the vision).** Paid services or outside calls by default: peer-to-peer and text-to-give, SMS, wealth screening providers, background check ordering APIs, e-signature, payment processing, auctions. Paid AI: fundraising assistants, AI cleanup and dedupe. Mass email and newsletters (Section 6.1.3; G-28 exports instead). Developer and metadata tools outside the plan's non-goals: metadata deploy and compare, anonymous Apex, API explorers, org-to-org sync, formula evaluator, bulk or desktop loaders, org-wide automation switches. Experience Cloud or per-login licensed portals (D-09, D-120). Hosted services and paid tiers (self-service only, D-147). Campaign dependencies outside Mirror. Board document annotation, video conferencing and PDF packet merge (third-party or not possible natively).

### 6.2 Priority rules used (so future re-prioritization stays consistent)

1. Anything Maria needs on day one (install, households, gifts, import) outranks everything.
2. Anything that protects the numbers (rollups, receipts, refunds as records) outranks convenience.
3. Features that serve the five market needs (Section 2.4) outrank features that are merely familiar from NPSP.
4. Compatibility adapters come after the native experience is solid; a mirror of a bad model is a bad mirror.
5. Every new package costs install friction; a package is justified only when its schema would clutter an org that does not need it.

---


### 6.3 Re-sequencing for the Agentforce Nonprofit first-customer focus (owner decision, 2026-09-07)

Because the first customers are Nonprofit Cloud / Agentforce Nonprofit orgs (Section 2.1), the following items move; the tables in Section 5 and 6 keep their original rows and this subsection governs where they differ:

- C-22 (junction membership hardening with Person Accounts) moves from v0.5 into v0.1 and v0.2: v0.1 ships junction mode as a working, tested path (Household_Member__c with Person Account members through dynamic field access, naming and greetings from Person Account name fields, sample data that loads as Person Accounts when they are enabled); v0.2 finishes hardening (merge, split, rollups through the junction).
- The Person Accounts org shape is the primary CI shape; a true Nonprofit Cloud scratch org shape (Industries features) is added as soon as the feature name is verified (owner follow-up).
- X-07 (Gift Transaction mirror) moves from v0.10 to v0.6; X-05 (NPSP coexistence: adopt household accounts) moves from v0.6 to v0.10. The NPSP org shape stays in CI but is not gating for v0.1 to v0.5 acceptance.
- Import templates: both sets, the NPC import templates (Nonprofit Cloud: Gift Transaction and related) and the NPSP import templates, ship together in v0.5 (owner decision, 2026-09-24). C-14 in v0.2 shipped only the generic donor and gift list templates; the NPC set was not built then.
- Every entity that references a person (Household Member, Gift donor, Soft Credit, Relationship, Affiliation, Address personal owner, Tribute honoree and recipient) carries both a Contact reference and an Account reference with the rule that exactly one is set, so Person Account people work everywhere without a compile-time dependency (Section 4.2 hard rules still apply: no Person Account field is referenced statically in Core).
- **Dependency rule reaffirmed by the owner (2026-09-07):** focusing on Nonprofit Cloud / Agentforce Nonprofit customers does not create a dependency on them. All core functionality depends only on this product and on functionality included with the Salesforce platform: no additional cloud, Industries object, permission set license, or add-on product is ever required (Principle 4, D-09). Integrations with other solutions (Nonprofit Cloud objects, NPSP, payment processors, accounting) live in the Mirror package or later adapters and are optional. Vendored open source code is included as source under the project license, never as a package dependency.

## 7. Engineering standards, quality, and definition of done

### 7.1 Naming conventions

- **Objects and fields:** `PascalCase__c`, module-neutral names (no module prefix in the API name because the namespace already disambiguates and prefixes make labels ugly). Labels are plain English a nonprofit uses: "Gift" not "Donation Transaction," "Fund" not "General Accounting Unit."
- **Apex:** one class per responsibility. Prefixes by role: `*Service` (business logic, the only place other modules may call), `*Selector` (SOQL), `*Domain` or `*TriggerHandler` (record-level rules), `*Controller` (LWC-facing, `@AuraEnabled`), `*Batch`, `*Queueable`, `*Schedulable`, `*Test`. Cross-package calls go through `global` service interfaces only.
- **LWC:** `npc` prefix folder naming not required (namespace handles it); component names describe the screen: `settingsConsole`, `householdNamingPreview`, `importWizard`.
- **Custom labels:** `Module_Screen_Purpose` (for example `Giving_ReceiptPage_IssuedStamp`).
- **Custom permissions:** `Manage_BarnCRM_Settings`, `Enter_Gifts`, `Issue_Receipts`, `Manage_Volunteers`, `Manage_Programs`, `Manage_Logic_Models`, `View_Funder_Pipeline`.

### 7.2 Code rules (CI-enforced where possible)

- Apex: `with sharing` by default; `WITH USER_MODE` or `Security.stripInaccessible` on every DML and query that touches user-facing data; bulkified; no SOQL or DML in loops; no hard-coded IDs; no `Test.isRunningTest()` branches in production logic.
- Every trigger goes through the trigger framework; one trigger per object; handlers are bypassable.
- Every `@AuraEnabled` method validates input and returns a typed result or a structured error the UI can render.
- Governor-limit safety proven by tests that insert 200 records per DML context.
- LWC: no third-party JS except vetted static resources. None is used today: SheetJS's pre-approval for import parsing is unused, since C-19 reads XLSX in the browser without it (ADR-0049). Record any addition as a decision. Jest tests for every component with logic.
- Salesforce Code Analyzer (PMD, ESLint, retire-js, Graph Engine for FLS/CRUD) must pass with zero high or critical findings on every pull request. This is the single best predictor of passing security review.
- Apex test coverage target 90% per package; 75% is the platform minimum and is not the target.

### 7.3 Testing strategy

- **Unit tests** per class, data created through a shared `TestDataFactory` in Core (exposed with `@NamespaceAccessible`, ADR-0017, so module packages can use it).
- **Org-shape matrix in CI:** every pull request runs tests on four scratch org definitions: Platform-only, Sales Cloud, Sales Cloud with NPSP installed, and Person Accounts enabled (simulating Agentforce Nonprofit shape; a true Nonprofit Cloud scratch org needs the Industries features flag, add it when available). Mirror's dynamic Apex is verified by the Platform-only shape where the objects are absent.
- **Upgrade tests:** install version N-1, load sample data, upgrade to N, run assertions. Automated from v0.3 onward.
- **Scale tests** (v0.11): 100k contacts, 40k households, 1M gifts, rollup full recalculation within platform limits, plus one account with 60k gifts (skew case) and a parallel Bulk API load of 50k gifts to 10 households (lock case); import of a 250k-row file. **Pilot bar (Brandon, 2026-10-14, D-90):** 25,000 rows is enough for the pilot; the 250k-row run comes later, in a sandbox. Rollup pass targets (proposed with D-16): nightly full recalculation of all 50 shipped defaults in under 2 hours with zero governor-limit failures; the parallel load ends with every total correct and no Failed queue rows after retries; one interactive gift save adds under 300 ms CPU; a second full recalculation writes 0 rows; the queue drains (50,000 gifts loaded in parallel are reflected in every total within 15 minutes, with the oldest queue entry's age recorded; ADR-0082).
- **Manual acceptance:** each feature has a scripted walkthrough in its admin-guide page that a human (Brandon) performs before the iteration is promoted. Playwright end-to-end tests are optional and post-0.6.

### 7.4 Definition of done (per feature)

- [ ] Canonical model updated first (Section 4.4).
- [ ] Metadata, Apex, LWC complete with tests: Jest and the `scripts/ci` checks pass on the feature PR; its relevant Apex tests pass in the incremental org gate (`barn-sales`) on the landing batch that carries it; and every Apex test passes, with the coverage floor, in the release gates (`barn-sales` and `oi-test`) before the version ships (D-141, D-142).
- [ ] Settings for the feature exposed in the Settings console with descriptions; no Setup step required for normal use.
- [ ] Permission sets updated; no new permission requires cloning.
- [ ] Error paths logged to Error Log with human-readable messages.
- [ ] Admin-guide page written (what it does, how to turn it on, a five-minute walkthrough, common mistakes).
- [ ] Documentation set updated in the same PR (D-116): the Admin Guide page, the User Guide page (if users touch the feature), the Testing Guide script(s) and, if the feature's status or the module list changes, `docs/overview.md`.
- [ ] Release note line written.
- [ ] Sample data updated if the feature adds objects.
- [ ] Reviewed by a second agent or human against this checklist (Section 9.4).

### 7.5 Release process

1. Merge to `main` with CI green.
2. Create a package version (`sf package version create`) for each changed package; beta versions for testing.
3. Install beta into the internal test orgs (`oi-test` and `oi-pa`) and the prerelease cohort.
4. Run upgrade tests and the manual walkthrough.
5. Promote (`sf package version promote`) and tag the repo `v0.N.0`.
6. Push upgrade to opted-in orgs (from v0.6; before that, manual installs). Push schedule: never on a Friday, never in the last week of December (year-end giving) or the first two weeks of January (year-end receipting).
7. Publish release notes written for Maria, not for developers.
8. Refresh What's New (`Release_Note__mdt`) and run `scripts/ci/check-whats-new.py --release`.

---

## 8. AppExchange and open source readiness

### 8.1 AppExchange path (start in v0.1, not v0.11)

- Join the Salesforce Partner Program as an ISV and obtain the Partner Business Org; use it as the Dev Hub for all promoted package versions. (Verify current program terms and whether the security review fee is waived for free listings; historically it has been reduced or waived for free apps, but confirm in writing.)
- Package versions must be created from the Dev Hub that will own the listing; creating early versions from a throwaway Dev Hub means re-creating the package with new IDs later. Decide the Dev Hub before v0.1's first package version.
- Security review requirements to design for from the start: Code Analyzer clean, FLS and CRUD enforcement, no secrets, external callouts documented (we make none by default), REST endpoints with authentication documented, a test org with sample data and credentials for the review team, and an architecture document (this file plus `docs/architecture/`).
- License Management App (LMA) is optional for a free app; install it anyway so install counts and versions are visible.
- Listing assets: short description, long description, five screenshots, a two-minute video, a demo org, support URL (GitHub Discussions), documentation URL, and a privacy statement (no data leaves the org; telemetry is opt-in and anonymous).

### 8.2 Open source governance

- **License:** Apache-2.0 recommended (patent grant protects contributors and adopters); BSD-3-Clause is the NPSP precedent and an acceptable alternative. Decided 2026-09-22 (Brandon): Apache-2.0, recorded as Decision D-08.
- **Contribution:** Developer Certificate of Origin (DCO sign-off on commits) rather than a CLA; lower friction, adequate protection for a free product.
- **Steward entity:** the project needs a legal owner for the namespace, the PBO, the AppExchange listing, and the trademark. Options are a nonprofit (a revived Nonprofit Collaborative is a natural fit and aligns with "given to nonprofits for free"), an LLC, or a fiscal sponsor. This is a decision for Brandon, not the builder; it must be made before the PBO is created because the PBO is tied to a legal entity.
- **Governance model:** BDFL (Brandon) for v0.x; a maintainers group with a documented decision process by v1.0; a public roadmap in GitHub Projects.
- **Community surfaces:** GitHub Issues and Discussions, a Trailblazer Community group, quarterly community calls once there are users.
- **Contributor experience targets:** clone to running scratch org with sample data in under ten minutes via one script; every module has a `README.md` with its objects, services, and how to test it alone; `good first issue` labels maintained.

### 8.3 Name and trademark

**Update 2026-09-23 (Brandon):** "Open Impact" is not available. The tentative new name is BarnCRM, covering two editions, then called the Impact Suite (nonprofits) and the Community Suite (for-profit and other organizations); renamed the Nonprofit Edition and the Standard Edition on 2026-10-14 (D-96). The same checks apply before any public use. The repository, labels and docs keep the working title until the name is verified, then are renamed in one pass.

**Name cleared 2026-10-12 (Brandon):** BarnCRM passed the checks below (trademark search and domains confirmed by Brandon), so the one-pass rename started the same day. Done 2026-10-12: this plan and BMemory, the GitHub repository (`Nonprofit-Collaborative/OpenImpact` renamed `Nonprofit-Collaborative/BarnCRM` after an empty placeholder repository of that name was deleted; GitHub redirects the old URL), the maintainer's local folder, and the repository docs (with an ADR recording the name). Update 2026-10-12 (later): the packaged rename below is done on branches `feature/barncrm-rename-1` to `-4`, stacked on C-29, and lands after it (Section 11.3 item 6). Originally still to do: packaged labels and API names (the app, "Open Impact Settings" and the other C-29 labels, the `Open_Impact_Giving` report and dashboard folders, the `Open_Impact_Acknowledgments` email folder and `Open_Impact_Thank_You` template). That waits until the C-29 stack lands, because C-29 introduced most of those labels; it is Section 11.3 work item 6. The namespace stays deferred.

**Name vetting behind BarnCRM (2026-09-23, Brandon with Claude).** About 900 candidates were screened by web search and DNS (not a legal clearance). Brandon's criteria: short, memorable, meaningful, natural (a word people already say; no suffix formulas such as -ly, no glued compounds, no uncommon words). His legal gate: any conflict, including a caution, rejects a name, because the project will not risk a legal dispute. Barn was the only strong name to pass. Story: the barn raising, where the whole community builds what no one could alone; also where you keep what matters and get work done. Positioning: simple and human, a calm contrast to AI-agent fatigue; pitched as simple, never as anti-AI, because the product lives on Salesforce's platform. Mascot: sea otters. Open items before public use: a full trademark search (USPTO classes 9, 35, 42, state and common law) with attention to Barnfox (a members app) and BARN OWL (computer services); register barncrm.org and barncrm.com (both showed no DNS on 2026-09-23); confirm the namespace in the Dev Hub; use "BarnCRM" consistently because "barn" alone is unsearchable. Rejected finalists, so they are not screened again: Raft (Raftr sells engagement software to nonprofits), Potluck (Potluck Inc software filing), Kindred (KINDRED software mark, Kindred CRM app), Hearth (Hearth sells CRM features), Meadow (MEADOW software registration, Meadow CRM), Kelp (Kelp Global CRM module), Nimbus (S-Nimbus AppExchange partner, TIBCO mark), Tend (TEND class 9 mark), Steward (Steward nonprofit CRM), Bandly (Naver BAND, getbandly.com), Barnly (barnly.app, barnly.life), Moyo (MOYO Salesforce partner), Comun (Común fintech), Impactly, OpenCRM; Skein, Everyhand, Manykin, Holdfast and the -ly coinages failed Brandon's taste; Stockpot, Bakesale, Clambake, Pledgehog and Lull passed the gate but scored lower.

**Re-opened 2026-09-06 (later the same day):** Brandon found that "Open Impact" is already in use, including by a foundation that funds open source work, which is too close to this project to share a name. "Open Impact" is now only a placeholder working title in the repository until a replacement is chosen and thoroughly vetted (USPTO, AppExchange, GitHub, npm, domains, social handles, Salesforce namespace). D-01 is therefore open again; the fallback "OpenCause" must go through the same vetting before use.

"Open Impact" was chosen by Brandon on 2026-09-06 after a web search found no Salesforce app, nonprofit software product, or consultancy of that name. Before any public repository, listing, or post: search the USPTO, the AppExchange listing directory, GitHub, npm, and domain registries; confirm the namespace is available. Fallback name if a conflict appears: OpenCause. Note that "impact" is heavily used across nonprofit technology, so the distinctiveness rests on the pair "Open Impact" and on a consistent visual identity. The namespace is immutable and the listing name is expensive to change.

## 9. Working agreements for the Claude Code session and sub-agents

This section exists because the builder is an AI session that will not have the context of the conversations that produced this plan.

### 9.1 First actions, in order (day one)

1. Read this document fully. Then read `docs/architecture/` if it exists.
2. Confirm with Brandon: license (D-08) and Dev Hub choice (Section 8.1). The namespace is deliberately deferred; build namespace-agnostic per Section 4.3 and do not create package versions until Brandon registers it.
3. Scaffold the monorepo exactly as Section 4.3 describes, with CI running an empty test suite on the test org and Code Analyzer as a required check.
4. Write `docs/architecture/canonical-model.md` for the v0.1 entities (Household, Household Member, Contact, Organization) before creating any object.
5. Write the v0.1 ADRs in `docs/architecture/decisions/` from Section 12.
6. Build v0.1 per Section 6, feature by feature, each on its own branch, each meeting the definition of done.
7. Stop at the end of v0.1 and report (Section 9.5) before starting v0.2.

### 9.2 How to split work across sub-agents

Split by **module and layer**, never by "front end vs back end" across the same feature, because the interface between them is the thing that most often breaks.

- One agent owns the **canonical model and object metadata** for an iteration and publishes the field list before anyone codes against it.
- One agent per **feature** (service, trigger handler, tests, LWC, admin-guide page) working against the published metadata.
- One agent owns **CI, org shapes, and packaging** for the iteration.
- One agent acts as **reviewer** against Section 7.4 and Section 2.3 for every pull request, and must try the feature as Maria with only the admin-guide page as instructions.
- Shared metadata files (permission sets, the settings console navigation, custom labels, layouts) are merge-conflict magnets: assign one agent to integrate those from the feature agents' requests rather than letting every agent edit them.
- Sub-agents (Brandon, 2026-10-30): use them only where most effective for this project, overriding the global delegate-by-default rule. Build in the main session when that likely uses fewer tokens; delegate where parallel work or a lean main context pays off. Use lower models (Sonnet, Haiku for trivial lookups) wherever appropriate to maximize utilization.

### 9.3 Rules for agents

- Use the personas' names in tests and docs.
- When a platform limitation blocks the plan, record an ADR with the workaround and continue; do not silently change the design.
- Never add a dependency on a paid product, an Industries object, or a standard object outside Mirror.
- Never put a setting in Setup that could be in the console.
- Prefer standard platform features over code (record types, dynamic forms, list views, standard reports, Flow for admin-editable logic) unless the standard feature cannot be packaged or would require Setup.
- Write the admin-guide page **before** the LWC; if the page is hard to write, the feature is too complicated.
- Keep pull requests under roughly 800 lines of change; larger ones are split.
- Every SOQL query is in a Selector; every DML that user input can reach is in user mode.
- Do not invent features. If something seems missing, add it to the parking lot (Section 11.3) with a sentence of justification and continue.

### 9.4 Review checklist (used by the reviewer agent and by Brandon)

- Does it meet the definition of done (Section 7.4)?
- Could Maria do this without Setup and without the docs? If not, why not, and is that recorded?
- Are the numbers right? (Rollups recalculated in tests after every path: insert, update, delete, undelete, reparent, merge.)
- Does it work on the Platform-only org shape?
- Does it degrade gracefully when a module is off or a standard object is absent?
- Is anything overwritten on upgrade that an admin might have changed?
- Is the label language a nonprofit's language?
- Are the Admin Guide page, the User Guide page (if users touch it), the Testing Guide script(s) and, if status or the module list changed, `docs/overview.md` updated in this PR, and does each index's "Applies to" line name a current main commit (D-116)?
- Does every class state its sharing and every query and DML its access mode (`check-apex-access-modes.py`), and does every test run as a permission-set user (`check-tests-run-as.py`)?

### 9.5 Reporting format (end of each iteration)

A short markdown report in `docs/release-notes/v0.N.md` with: features completed against the plan, features deferred and why, ADRs added, test counts and coverage per package, org shapes passing, open risks, and the exact steps for Brandon to install and walk through the iteration in a fresh org. Brandon will review once per iteration; do not wait for him mid-iteration unless blocked by Section 9.1 item 2 or a decision in Section 11.4.

---


### 9.6 Concurrent iterations (owner decision, 2026-09-07)

Brandon directed the build session to run features from several iterations at once rather than waiting for each iteration's package version to be promoted (Section 6 sequencing rule relaxed because no Dev Hub or package versions exist yet and the owner is handling DevOps separately). Rules that still hold: the canonical model for an iteration's entities is published before its feature agents start; each feature is its own branch and pull request, reviewed against 7.4 and 9.4, and lands on `main` through a squash-merged landing batch when it passes (D-141); features from a later iteration may merge to `main` before the earlier iteration has been accepted, because `main` is unreleased. Each iteration is tagged (for example `v0.1.0-review`) when its features are complete so Brandon can QA that exact state in a fresh scratch org while `main` moves on; the iteration's release notes point at the tag. Package versions are still created only after the namespace and Dev Hub decisions.

Standing owner instructions for the build session (recorded 2026-10-12 from the session's working notes):
- **Merge authority (Brandon, 2026-09-23):** "Merge anything when you decide it is ready and doesn't need anything and doesn't have a reason why it shouldn't." In practice: squash-merge without asking when every required check (Static checks, DCO sign-off check, Org tests (local)) is green on the head commit, review threads are resolved, and nothing is open or questionable. Hold and ask when an owner question, a failing or skipped check, or a design doubt is involved. Report merges afterwards.
- **Test orgs (Brandon, 2026-09-23, 2026-09-24 and 2026-10-12):** use only `oi-test`, `oi-pa` and `barn-core` (a temporary Sales Cloud scratch org for Core-only coverage, approved by Brandon 2026-10-12; Section 11.2 item 26); creating any other scratch org needs Brandon's approval first, and recreating `oi-pa` from `config/scratch-defs/person-accounts.json` when it expires is pre-approved. Agents share each org one deploy or test run at a time. Never read, print or store an org auth URL, and never dispatch the GitHub org-test workflow.
- **Sandbox `mwdbdevsb` (Brandon, 2026-10-12):** approved for testing the Standard Edition (Core without Giving). It is a sandbox of a real, built-out org, not a dummy org, so it is never the project's default target org; every deploy is a check-only deploy first and runs only with Brandon's go-ahead; no data change, destructive deploy or scheduled job without his approval; deploy Core only, unless he says otherwise. **Amended 2026-10-25 (Brandon):** the sandbox is tested as it is, with BarnCRM added on top; never change its org settings (no Person Accounts enablement or similar); the testers Brandon Mead and Michelle Hansen get every BarnCRM permission needed to review and test. 2026-10-26 (Brandon): the sandbox is metadata-only (0 Contacts, 0 Accounts), so BarnCRM sample data is loaded there after the deploy for testing. Brandon, 2026-10-29: standing approval to stop and reschedule the BarnCRM Tab Access Check (and other BarnCRM scheduled jobs) for every mwdbdevsb deploy he has approved.
- **Gates (Brandon, 2026-10-26, D-131; extends D-56; batch testing 2026-10-28, D-141; incremental and release gates 2026-10-28, D-142):** "I want the testing to be done in batches and optimized to go faster wherever prudent," for everything in this project going forward, and "I'd mostly like these initial tests to be as fast as possible with an end-to-end test of the entire new version once a significant new version's initial development is done and integrated. I don't want as much testing for all the incremental changes." Feature PRs run Static checks, DCO, local Jest and the `scripts/ci` checks only, with no org gate. An integrator merges every ready feature branch into one `landing/batch-N` branch, resolves conflicts once, refreshes `docs/product-plan.md` from BMemory once and writes the root docs, then runs the incremental org gate on `barn-sales` on the landing head: the staged deploy (unchanged packages skipped) and RunRelevantTests on the changed Apex plus the critical test classes, with no coverage floor. Failures are fixed on the landing branch; the landing PR is squash-merged only after the gate passes, and the included feature PRs are closed as landed. Accepted risk: the relevant-test selection can miss indirect paths and non-Apex changes. Once a significant version's development is done and integrated, the release gates (`--release`) run every test with the coverage floor on `barn-sales` and `oi-test` in parallel (each org has its own lock); then the version is re-validated and deployed to `barn-npc` and `mwdbdevsb` (each `mwdbdevsb` deploy needs Brandon's approval), then QA and UX tested. A third scratch org, `barn-sales` (Sales Cloud shape), is approved for gates.
- **Gate from a clean checkout:** run `scripts/org/run-org-tests.sh` only from a dedicated worktree checked out detached at the pushed head, never from a worktree someone is editing; the script checks for a clean tree only when it starts, so mid-run edits get deployed half-finished.
- **Core only until the modules start (Brandon, 2026-10-13; D-84):** "Going forward, please only present findings and decisions related to core (not the extra modules) until I start working on those modules. The feedback, decisions, etc, can be recorded in relation to those modules in the project plan or somewhere appropriately so they can be referenced when I'm working on those modules. Right now I want to get Core v1.0 completed." Module findings go to Section 11.5, grouped by module, not into reports to Brandon.
- **Implement recommendations (Brandon, 2026-10-13; D-85):** "Going forward, please implement your recommendations and just tell me what's being changed unless it's something really important or contrary to something I've asked for or instructed." Clarification the same day: "Reviews or audits already in flight related to module can finish, I just don't want to process through their findings right now if they're not related to Core."
- **Synced checkout:** the maintainer's checkout sits in a synced folder that creates untracked "name 2.ext" copies (including inside `.git/`). Stage by explicit path, never `git add -A`, and check `git show --stat` for ` 2.` files before pushing.

### 9.7 Release plan upkeep (owner decision, 2026-10-31, D-155)

Release management is automatic; Brandon is not asked to prompt it. The session that causes a change updates Section 6.0a (BMemory first, then `docs/product-plan.md` and the root `roadmap.md`) in the same session:

- Every newly approved item is placed in a release (about 3 major and 3 to 6 minor enhancements each), ordered by Brandon's sequencing, Core before modules, dependencies, then impact. Unapproved ideas stay in the "Not approved" list; C-38 stays held until Brandon starts it. New features consistent with the vision may be added and placed without his approval (D-156); new modules or editions, reversals of settled decisions or non-goals, and not-approved items go to him as proposals.
- When Brandon asks to move a feature up or back, it is re-slotted and the affected releases are rebalanced to stay near the size rule; the change is reported in one line.
- Statuses (Planned, In progress, Landed, Shipped) update as feature branches start, landing batches merge and batches ship.
- Each landing batch covers 2 or 3 releases. Feature PRs and incremental gates run as in Section 9.6 (D-141, D-142); the release gate (`--release` on `barn-sales` and `oi-test`), the `barn-npc` and `mwdbdevsb` deploys (Brandon's approval for each `mwdbdevsb` deploy) and QA and UX testing run once per batch, after the whole-solution review (Section 9.8). Each release ships its What's New record.

### 9.8 Whole-solution review before each batch test (owner decision, 2026-10-31)

Brandon, 2026-10-31: "Before each batch is tested, please review the entire solution for QA, UX, and for redundancies/simplifications that may reduce the code base, reduce complexity, or otherwise improve performance. None of these changes should remove functionality or usefulness. This should be an opportunity to remove unused/deprecated elements and to merge elements as would be recommended for optimized simplicity. Please make sure this process is done with the end result in mind (to make things as simple as possible but not too simple)."

- When a batch's builds are integrated and before its release gate runs, review all packages (Core and modules), not only the batch's changes, along three lines: QA (defects, test gaps), UX (consistency, copy, accessibility, navigation), and simplification (unused or deprecated components, duplicate logic, mergeable classes, components or settings, performance).
- Every change keeps all functionality and usefulness; removals are limited to elements nothing uses or that a merged element fully replaces. Judge each against the end state: as simple as possible, but not simpler.
- Findings that fit these rules are fixed in the batch before testing; anything that would remove a capability, change settled architecture or reverse a decision goes to Brandon as a recommendation.
- Removed metadata is listed for the destructive steps of the barn-npc and mwdbdevsb deploys.

### 9.9 Portability toward BarnCRM for WordPress (owner guidance, 2026-10-31)

Brandon, 2026-10-31: "I may later want to re-create a version of all of this as BarnCRM for WordPress (likely to be used alongside my Pegwork for WordPress solution) so please keep that future compatibility in mind if there are ways to build things in a more universal way (ex. LWCs using http, css, and js rather than APEX)." Platform: [Pegwork](/specification/wordpress-platform-plugin.md) (schema registry as JSON, REST, block rendering, automation builder).

Applied as a design preference, never at the cost of the Salesforce edition's security, limits, or the Section 4 architecture:

- **Logic in plain JavaScript where it can safely run in the browser:** parsing, header and value normalization, fuzzy matching, transforms, validation previews and formatting live in framework-free ES modules (no `lightning/*` or `@salesforce/*` imports) that a WordPress block or Node can reuse. Data access, sharing, permissions, DML and anything that must be enforced stay server-side in Apex.
- **Configuration as data:** templates, field maps, transform steps, rules, match settings and seed data are stored or exportable as plain JSON with documented schemas, so they can map onto Pegwork's registry.
- **Shared test vectors:** logic that exists in both Apex and JS is tested against the same JSON fixtures.
- **Thin Apex controllers** that return plain JSON shapes (no Salesforce-only types leaking into the UI contract), so the same UI could call a WordPress REST endpoint.
- **Styling through a small BarnCRM token layer** on top of SLDS styling hooks, so a block theme can supply the same tokens; copy in labels so it exports as translatable strings.
- **Canonical model stays platform-neutral** (`docs/architecture/canonical-model.md`): names and relationships describe the domain, not Salesforce mechanics.

The pre-batch review (9.8) checks new work against this list; existing code is moved toward it only where the change is cheap and keeps behavior.

## 10. v0.1 in detail (the first build)

### 10.1 User stories

- As Maria, I want to install one package and land on a home page that tells me what to do next, so that I never need to search for where to start.
- As Maria, I want new contacts to get a household automatically with a sensible name, so that I do not create households by hand.
- As Maria, I want to preview how household names and greetings will look before I commit to a naming rule, so that my mail merges are right the first time.
- As David, I want to override a specific household's name and greeting and have the system stop recomputing it, so that "The Reverend and Mrs. Smith" stays that way.
- As Maria, I want to move a contact to another household or merge two households from the record page, so that data cleanup takes minutes.
- As Maria, I want to give a colleague access by picking their name and a role, so that I never have to learn permission sets.
- As Maria, I want to pause an automation that is causing a problem during an import, so that I am never stuck.
- As Maria, I want to see errors in plain language in the app, so that I can fix them or ask for help with specifics.
- As Maria, when my org already has NPSP or Person Accounts, I want the app to notice and set itself up compatibly, so that I do not break what I have.
- As Sam, I want to clone the repo and have a scratch org with sample data in ten minutes, so that I can contribute.

### 10.2 Acceptance criteria (representative; the builder extends per feature)

**C-01 Household model**
- Given a fresh Platform-only org with the package installed, when a Contact is inserted with no Account, then a Household Account with record type Household is created and the Contact is attached, within the same transaction.
- Given junction mode is active and Person Accounts are enabled, when a Person Account is created, then no Household is created automatically unless the setting "Create households for person accounts" is on; when it is on, a `Household_Member__c` record links the Person Account to a new Household.
- Given a Contact is reparented to a different Household, then both households' membership counts and (once Giving is installed) giving rollups are recalculated, and the previous household is deleted if empty and the "delete empty households" setting is on.
- Given 200 Contacts are inserted in one DML, then all households are created without hitting limits.

**C-02 Naming and greetings**
- Given two members with surname Smith, then Household Name is "The Smith Family" (or the configured pattern), Formal Greeting uses configured salutations, and Informal Greeting is "John and Jane."
- Given members with differing surnames, then the name is "Smith and Jones Household" and greetings list both.
- Given a member marked deceased, then they are excluded from greetings and, if the setting is on, the name is unchanged.
- Given `Custom_Name__c` is checked on a Household, then no automatic recomputation changes Name, or a Formal Greeting or Informal Greeting that has a value (R-H8). Household Upkeep and the recompute batch (Update All Household Names) fill only a blank greeting from the members, with the same patterns and rules as any other household.
- Given the admin changes the naming pattern in the console, then a preview shows five sample households and a "Recompute all households" action runs as a batch with progress and a completion notice.

**C-03 Hub and Settings console**
- Given a user without `Manage_BarnCRM_Settings`, when they open BarnCRM Settings, then they see a read-only view with a message naming the permission.
- Given a setting is changed, then it takes effect on the next transaction without cache staleness beyond ten seconds, and the change is logged (who, when, old, new).
- Given the console search box, when "greeting" is typed, then the household naming settings appear.

**C-06 Access page**
- Given Maria selects a user and the role "Fundraising Staff," then the corresponding permission set group is assigned and the user appears in the role's list; removal works the same way.

**C-04 Automation control**
- Given "Pause all automation for 2 hours" is activated, then no packaged trigger logic runs for the org until expiry or manual resume, and the Hub shows a banner while paused.

**C-11 Health Check**
- Given NPSP is installed, then the Health Check reports "NPSP detected" and the coexistence setting defaults to NPSP coexistence with an explanation.

### 10.3 v0.1 metadata inventory (starting point; the canonical model governs)

- Account record types: Household only, never given to users (BarnCRM creates households in system mode); organizations use a record type the org already has, or none (D-134). Contact: no packaged record type (owner decision, Brandon, 2026-09-23). The former Household Contact record type drove no behavior, and an org can add its own Contact record types without one.
- Account fields: `Household_Formal_Greeting__c`, `Household_Informal_Greeting__c`, `Custom_Name__c`, `Primary_Contact__c`, `Member_Count__c`, `Anniversary__c`.
- Contact fields: `Deceased__c`, `Household_Role__c` (Head, Spouse or Partner, Child, Other), `Exclude_From_Household_Name__c`, `Exclude_From_Greetings__c`, `Preferred_Name__c`.
- Objects: `Household_Member__c` (junction, used only in junction mode), `Error_Log__c`, `Automation_Setting__c` (or custom metadata plus custom setting per D-06), `Setting_Change__c` (audit).
- Custom settings: `Nonprofit_Settings__c` (hierarchy, protected).
- Custom metadata: `Automation_Registry__mdt`. (`Naming_Pattern__mdt` was removed on 2026-09-22, before the first package version: nothing read it, and naming reads the pattern fields on BarnCRM Settings.)
- Apex: `HouseholdService`, `HouseholdNamingService`, `HouseholdSelector`, `ContactTriggerHandler`, `AccountTriggerHandler`, `TriggerDispatcher`, `AutomationControl`, `ErrorLogger`, `SettingsService`, `AccessService`, `HealthCheckService`, `OrgShapeDetector`, `SampleDataLoader`, `TestDataFactory`, tests for each.
- LWC: the Hub tiles (`hubWelcome`, `hubSetup`, `hubErrors`, `hubRollups`, `hubSeasonalAddresses`, `hubRecurringFiles`, `hubModules`, `hubQuickLinks`; `hubHome` was replaced by them), `setupAssistant`, `settingsConsole`, `settingsSearch`, `householdNamingSettings` (with preview), `householdMembersPanel` (record page), `householdMergeSplit`, `accessManager`, `automationControl`, `errorLogTile`, `healthCheckPanel`.
- Permission sets: `BarnCRM_Admin`, `BarnCRM_Staff`, `BarnCRM_Read_Only`, `BarnCRM_Duplicate_Review`, `BarnCRM_Find_And_Export`, `BarnCRM_Bulk_Update`, `BarnCRM_Bulk_Delete` and `BarnCRM_Metadata_Admin`; group per role.
- App: `BarnCRM` with tabs Home, Households, Contacts, Organizations, Import, Find, Bulk Update, Data Hygiene, BarnCRM Settings, Error Log (admin only). The Rollup Builder tab belongs to the BarnCRM Rollup Builder module (D-101, landed #438) and lives in the BarnCRM admin apps, with no separate app (D-130); rollup settings are in BarnCRM Settings, Rollups section (the Rollup Settings tab is removed, D-126).
