## 11. Risks, open questions, and parking lot

### 11.1 Risks

| Risk | Likelihood | Impact | Mitigation |
|---|---|---|---|
| Namespace or Dev Hub chosen wrong early, forcing package re-creation | Medium | High | Blocking decision before any package version (Section 9.1) |
| Custom `Gift__c` instead of Opportunity alienates NPSP-dependent apps and consultants | Medium | Medium | Opportunity mirror (X-01) in v0.6; document clearly; the Platform-license win is worth it |
| Security review takes months and finds structural issues | Medium | High | Code Analyzer gate from v0.1; user-mode everywhere; review-readiness in v0.11, not at the end |
| Multi-package install friction confuses Maria | High | Medium | Module Manager (C-24); Core plus Giving may ship as a bundled install link; measure time-to-first-gift in beta |
| Rollup engine correctness bugs damage trust | Medium | Very high | Own engine built tests first (D-16): an engine-agnostic regression suite and a clean comparison run against apex-rollup's results before it is removed; recompute design, correct by construction; nightly Both safety net; extended v0.11 scale test (Section 7.3). Since S8 (2026-10-12) every rollup runs on the own engine; the risk closes when S9 removes apex-rollup after `RollupEngineComparison` runs clean on a test org |
| Salesforce changes the platform or Power of Us terms | Low | High | No Industries dependency; Platform-license floor is the hedge |
| Solo maintainer burnout; project stalls | High | High | Small modules; contributor guide from day one; steward entity; scope discipline (Section 1.5) |
| Person Account orgs behave differently in subtle ways | High | Medium | Dedicated CI org shape; junction mode isolated behind `HouseholdService` |
| Year-end receipt bug during December | Low | Very high | Release freeze windows (Section 7.5); receipt engine tests with fiscal-year fixtures |

### 11.2 Open questions for Brandon (blocking marked with **B**)

1. Namespace registration (deferred by Brandon; blocks package version creation only, not development).
2. **B** License: Apache-2.0 or BSD-3-Clause. **Resolved 2026-09-22 (Brandon): Apache-2.0, recorded as Decision D-08.**
3. **B** Dev Hub: Partner Business Org now, or Developer Edition Dev Hub with a planned migration before the first promoted version.
4. Steward entity: Nonprofit Collaborative, a new entity, or personal for v0.x.
5. Should Core and Giving ship as one package for v1.0 (fewer installs) with Volunteers, Programs, Funders, and Mirror separate? Current plan says separate; the argument for merging is that nearly every org needs both. Recommendation: keep separate in the repo, decide at v0.5 based on install-friction evidence. **Resolved 2026-09-23 (Brandon): separate.** The Standard Edition (then called the Community Suite) is Core without Giving, and the guided install from the Setup Assistant (C-30) removes most of the install friction.
6. PDF generation for receipts: Visualforce render-as-PDF (mature, packageable, limited styling) versus browser print from LWC (better styling, no stored file without a callout). Recommendation: Visualforce render-as-PDF for stored, immutable receipts; decide at v0.4. **Update 2026-09-07 (ADR-0016, Proposed):** research confirmed the Visualforce rendering engine but found that `getContentAsPDF()` counts as a callout (ten documents per batch execute, unusable in triggers and tests), while `Blob.toPdf()` uses the same rendering service as plain Apex since the Summer '26 release update. Recommendation now: `Blob.toPdf()` behind a `ReceiptRenderer` seam, Visualforce page as fallback; OmniStudio Document Generation rejected (add-on license); LWC print rejected (no stored bytes). Two spikes gated before v0.4: batch metering of `Blob.toPdf()` and logo or signature image delivery to the renderer. **Resolved 2026-09-08: ADR-0016 accepted by the owner, which settles the v0.4 engineering prerequisite in Section 6.** The decision stands as proposed: `Blob.toPdf()` over package-composed HTML behind a `ReceiptRenderer` seam, with the Visualforce `renderAs="pdf"` page as the named fallback. The two spikes are not closed and cannot be until a Salesforce org is available, so G-13 is built to the accepted design with the fallback path kept viable, and both spikes are carried as open items on the feature rather than as blockers: ADR-0016 records that a receipt is legally valid with no images at all, so image delivery cannot block the feature.
7. Telemetry: include opt-in anonymous usage at all? Recommendation: yes, default off, disclosed on the listing. **Resolved 2026-10-12 (Brandon): deferred indefinitely, since there is no AppExchange listing for now; recorded as Decision D-24.**
8. **B** How a household names its primary person, once people can be accounts. Found by the household model review on 2026-09-09. Canonical rule R-M3 says the member marked Is Primary is mirrored to the household's Primary Contact. Nothing implements it, and nothing can as written: `Account.Primary_Contact__c` is a lookup to Contact, so it cannot name a Person Account at all. A household composed entirely of person accounts therefore has no primary contact permanently, and member ordering loses the tiebreak that R-H5 gives it. Options: add a second lookup to Account beside the existing one (two fields for one idea, but both stay reportable); make the primary a question only `HouseholdService` answers, reading the junction in junction mode and the field in contact mode (consistent with R-H3 and R-M5, and the reason this is the recommendation); or drop the mirror from R-M3 and let the two modes differ openly. The cost of the recommendation is real and should decide it: a lookup field is reportable and a service method is not, so any report or formula that names the primary person would need the field to stay for contact mode and would have nothing in junction mode. This is escalation territory under Section 11.4, which names changing the household membership abstraction. Recorded as a known gap in `docs/architecture/canonical-model.md` Section 30 in the meantime, so the rule and the code no longer disagree silently. **Resolved provisionally 2026-09-23 (Brandon delegated the choice, to be confirmed when he tests): none of the three options.** A Person Account has a Contact record of its own (its person contact), and a lookup to Contact can name it, so R-M3 is kept as written with the one existing field. `HouseholdService` stays the only writer: in contact mode it mirrors the primary Contact, and in junction mode the primary member's Contact, or, when the member is a Person Account, that account's person contact, read through dynamic Apex only in orgs that have Person Accounts, so Core keeps no dependency on them. One field, reportable in both modes; the known gap in canonical model Section 30 closes when C-22 ships.

9. **B** Party Relationship Group coexistence and migration. Found by the Sales Cloud and Nonprofit Cloud readiness audit on 2026-09-15. Today nothing stops the naming batch from renaming an Account that carries a native household group; the delete guard (ADR-0036) covers deletion only. Proposed design: one Account has exactly one household model (BarnCRM never writes the name, greetings or address of an Account that carries a native group, and never auto-creates a duplicate for a person already in one), a drift report rather than any sync, and a supervised, reversible, one-time migration in Mirror as feature X-09 that never deletes a native group, a membership relation or an Account. No two-way sync, because a computed name and a hand-edited name have no convergence rule. Roughly eight developer-days for the guards in Core and twelve for the migration in Mirror; needs two ADRs and an `Account.Household_Model__c` field. Update 2026-09-15: detection and the four Core guards (naming on all three callers, auto-creation, address propagation, Health Check info plus collision warning) are built on `feature/household-readiness` (PR 41), using the weaker "is the person's account a native household" rule for auto-creation because native membership lives on an object Core may not query. Decision still open: guards only, or guards plus the Mirror migration. **Resolved provisionally 2026-09-23 (Brandon delegated the choice, to be confirmed when he tests): guards only.** The guards already prevent the damage; the migration (about twelve developer-days) waits in the parking lot until a real org with native household groups asks for it.
10. **B** Changing the membership mode on an org that already holds household data. No migration exists in either direction. Junction to Contact makes every membership row invisible, so the next upkeep recounts households to zero, renames them to the placeholder, and deletes them when empty-household deletion is on. Contact to Junction strands everyone already on a household, because `withoutAHousehold` skips them and they never get a membership row. A guard that refuses a switch that would strand data, with a Health Check finding, is being added on 2026-09-15. Decision: keep the guard only, or build the migration both ways. **Resolved provisionally 2026-09-23 (Brandon delegated the choice, to be confirmed when he tests): keep the guard only.** A switch that would strand data is refused, with a Health Check finding that says why. If an org needs to switch, the first migration to build is contact mode to junction mode (an org that turns on Person Accounts after adopting), and only when one asks.
11. Professional Edition. **Resolved 2026-09-15 (Brandon): Professional Edition and below are not a target. Nothing has to be compatible with them, so the platform-event and permission-set-group concerns below are moot and no edition check is added to CI.** Kept for the audit trail: nothing in the repository verifies it. Field, object, record type and validation rule counts sit comfortably inside its limits; the exposure is feature availability. Permission set groups and custom platform events may be unavailable there, and `Error_Log_Event__e` is the entire error-logging path, so error logging could fail silently on that edition. Verified on 2026-09-15 from search snippets of the ISVforce Guide and release notes, not a direct read, so medium confidence: permission set groups work in that edition and packaged ones do not count against its cap of ten; objects and tabs from a security-reviewed package listed on AppExchange are exempt from its limits, which makes the exemption conditional on the listing, not on being open source; and `ErrorLogWriter` already falls back to a direct insert of `Error_Log__c` whenever publishing throws, so at runtime logging degrades rather than going silent. The one thing that could not be settled from documentation is whether a package that contains a platform event can install into Professional Edition at all, since the ISVforce Guide says platform events are not supported there. If that blocks installation, the whole package fails on that edition and the fix is to make the event optional in Core. Only a real Professional Edition trial org can answer it.

30. Portal Builder (PB, D-118) login architecture. Direction set (Brandon, 2026-10-25): "For the board portal, I'd like a solution similar to Llama Sites or Titan portals that don't require Salesforce licenses for logins." Requirement added (Brandon, 2026-10-26; D-120): "There needs to be at least one option to build with Salesforce with logins but without additional cost." Board members, and the users of every other portal template, log in at no additional Salesforce license cost. Stays open only for confirming (B) as the build path after the terms check below; not blocking until Portal Builder work starts. An ADR is needed before build because the choice touches D-09, ADR-0013 and the Section 1.5 non-goal on portals. Options (research: Section 6.1.2 item 12):
    - **(B) On-platform Salesforce Site, required as the no-cost Salesforce option (D-120).** A Salesforce Site (Enterprise and Unlimited editions include Sites) with BarnCRM's own login layer in Apex: a one-time code by email, an optional second factor, server-side session records, and every request re-checks the session and the person's portal access before any system-mode read; guest-user sharing is kept at the minimum. No hosting and no added license. LlamaSite (native on Salesforce) most likely uses this pattern (UNVERIFIED: its docs do not say). Risks: guest-user security rules force system-mode Apex to serve confidential documents, so the per-request checks are the security boundary; Salesforce's contract terms on custom logins for Site guest access must be checked before build (UNVERIFIED; authenticated access is normally Experience Cloud); and it will face hard AppExchange security review scrutiny.
    - **(A) Off-platform portal, Titan-style, an optional later connector.** Titan uses this pattern (Section 6.1.2 item 12: https://support.titandxp.com/platform/salesforce/integration-connect, https://titandxp.com/?p=12588). A web front end hosted outside Salesforce (natural fit: the planned BarnCRM for WordPress edition, Section 6.1 item 16) with its own login (one-time code by email, optional two-factor), which verifies the person against their BarnCRM record; one Salesforce integration user (JWT) does the reads and writes through the API, so Salesforce data access is limited to what that integration user can see. Clearest security boundary, but the customer hosts it, and Salesforce's terms on serving many people through one integration user must be checked before build (UNVERIFIED).
    - **(C) Donated internal licenses, for small boards.** Qualified nonprofits get 10 Nonprofit Cloud Enterprise Edition licenses free through Power of Us (source: https://www.salesforce.com/en-us/wp-content/uploads/sites/4/Salesforce-for-Nonprofits-Pricing-Guide.pdf), so board members can be ordinary internal users at no cost while seats last. Nonprofits only; needs no portal.
31. Multi-brand management (C-47, D-119): should brand separation ever restrict who sees records (sharing), or only label and present them? Raised 2026-10-26; not blocking (planned after v1.0). Recommendation: label and present by default, with optional documented standard sharing rules (criteria-based sharing rules or public groups per brand) for organizations that need separation; no custom sharing code. **Resolved 2026-10-26 (Brandon): "There should be an option to restrict record visibility based on the brand(s) assigned to the record and what brand(s) the user has access to." An optional brand-restricted visibility setting, off by default, kept by share rows BarnCRM maintains (Section 6.1 item 17); the no-custom-sharing recommendation is superseded because standard sharing rules cannot express many-to-many brand membership. Recorded as Decision D-120; the sharing code needs an ADR before build.**

### 11.3 Parking lot (good ideas, deliberately not now)

- Donor portal and online giving (license and PCI burden).
- Email marketing integration beyond export.
- ~~Grantmaking as a funder (NPC does this well; different customer).~~ **Reversed 2026-10-26 (Brandon, D-122):** now the planned Scholarships and Outbound Grants module (Section 5.11).
- Peer-to-peer fundraising.
- Wealth screening integration.
- Text-to-give.
- Multi-org or affiliate roll-up reporting.
- An "AI assistant" inside the app (the clean model is the AI strategy for v1).
- Confidential interaction notes (Brandon, 2026-10-12; D-19): not built. Interaction notes (C-39) stay completed standard Tasks that follow the visibility of the record they are on; a note hidden from people who can see the record would need its own sharing model.
- **Salvage branch (recorded 2026-10-12):** branch `claude/openimpact-barncrm-status-ft0c1x` (2026-09-28) held the C-35 bulk update and X-07 Gift Transaction mirror work plus earlier C-33 work. **Update 2026-10-17 (branch review):** C-33 and C-35 landed separately in #219 (ADR-0077), so that part is superseded. Only X-07 is still unique there: commits `2baee967` (design), `5be47f99` (code) and `69199281` (tests), about 5,600 lines (Connect classes, the `giftTransactionMirror` LWC, labels, `docs/admin-guide/gift-transaction-mirror.md`, a canonical model section). When X-07 is built, cherry-pick those three onto a branch from `main` and port them to Mirror (`packages/mirror`) and the BarnCRM names; then delete the branch. Also kept from the review: `feature/v-01-v-02-v-03-volunteers` (2026-09-09, unfinished V-01 to V-03 objects, selectors and handlers; design input for the Volunteers module, predates the rename and neutral Core) and `feature/rollup-s15-spike` (2026-10-12, the S15 spike under `spikes/rollup-s15`; reference until the S16 decision, D-64).
- **Superseded 2026-09-23 (Brandon)** by Section 3, "Suites and packaging": the Standard Edition (then the Community Suite) is Core itself, made neutral by C-29, not a CI-built subset package. The earlier direction is kept for the record: A community Households edition for organizations that are not nonprofits (direction set by Brandon, 2026-09-22; not before Core's first package version). Nonprofits stay on one Core install with no extra step; Core is the primary product and is never split into a base package it depends on. The community edition is a separate unlocked package, installed from a link rather than listed as the nonprofit product, built by CI from Core's household source (a manifest names the shared files; nothing is maintained twice), with its own neutral labels. Packages start as unlocked packages with a registered namespace (Brandon, 2026-09-22), so a later move to managed keeps API names. Both editions use the same neutral namespace: several unlocked packages may share one, Salesforce recommends it, and it keeps the household objects' API names identical so conversion moves no data between objects. Whether Core can take ownership of the community edition's components in place is to be proven in a scratch org before the edition ships. One-way conversion from community to Core is a hidden or manual Setup Assistant step in Core that copies household data into Core's model, written with instructions when the edition exists; no conversion the other way.
- **Engineering follow-ups from the v0.5 and v0.6 reviews (recorded 2026-09-27, Brandon: "make sure they are detailed").** Not scheduled into an iteration. **Update, later 2026-10-12:** follow-ups 1, 3 and 4 merged with the rename stack (#181, squash `87a00cb9`); follow-up 5 (#203) landed in #219. **Status earlier 2026-10-12:** follow-ups 1, 3, 4 and 5 are built on open PRs, not yet merged: follow-up 1 on #188 (`feature/fu-1-settings-writers-1`, settings saves write only changed keys under a lock) and #189 (`feature/fu-1-settings-writers-2`, Giving first saves keep the default-on checkboxes); follow-up 3 on #190 (`feature/fu-3-dynamic-apex-1`, Health Check detects Connect by a class it ships), #191 (`feature/fu-3-dynamic-apex-2`, capped counts, cached describes, logged wrong-shape modules) and #192 (`feature/fu-3-dynamic-apex-3`, CI Person Account field gate, Connect declarations, Campaign-absent seam); follow-up 4 on #193 (`feature/fu-4-cli-heap`); follow-up 5 on #203 (`fix/review-follow-ups-5`, Opportunity mirror and receipt cap). Follow-up 2 (log unrecognized checkbox values in imports) has no branch yet. Each is small enough for one PR under the usual rules (admin guide first where behavior changes, tests including a 200-row bulk case where records are touched, Code Analyzer clean). The repo copy of this list is the "Handoff: open work and what still needs testing" section of `docs/contributor-guide/ci.md`; remove an item from both when its PR merges.
  1. **Harden the settings writers and cap uncapped counts.** Why: a settings save can silently overwrite a concurrent admin's change, and Giving can switch three default-on features off the first time it saves.
     - `SettingsService.applyValues`, through `SettingsWriter.saveOrgDefault`, upserts the whole cached, unlocked `Nonprofit_Settings__c` record, so every field is written back from a possibly stale copy. Fix: update only the fields that changed, through `OrgSettingsSystemWriter` with the row read by `SettingsRecordSelector.lockStored()` (`FOR UPDATE`), per ADR-0021 and ADR-0035.
     - Giving makes raw first saves from `Giving_Settings__c.getOrgDefaults()` in `AcknowledgmentService` (about lines 426 to 435), `DonorLevelWriter` (about 26 to 33), `AccountingPeriodWriter` (line 22), `AcknowledgmentController` (line 179) and `CommitmentService` (lines 595 and 607). When no org default row exists yet, `getOrgDefaults()` returns field defaults of false, so the first save writes false into the checkboxes that ship true: `Auto_Apply_Gifts_To_Installments__c`, `Automatic_Household_Soft_Credits__c`, `Receipt_Print_Logo__c`. Fix: route every first save through the path that applies shipped defaults in Apex (ADR-0035), and add a test per writer that starts with no org default row and asserts the three checkboxes stay true.
     - `SettingsService.getSettingsForUpdate()` takes no lock despite its name; rename it (for example `getSettings`) so no caller assumes a lock, and use `lockStored()` where a lock is needed.
     - `HealthCheckSettingsChecks.countNew()` runs an uncapped `COUNT()`; cap it (`LIMIT` plus "N or more" wording) so a large error backlog cannot hit query-row limits.
  2. **Log unrecognized checkbox values in imports.** Why: today any value other than true, yes or 1 silently becomes false, so "Y", "x" or "checked" in a file clears a box with no trace. `ImportRowProcessor.convertCheckbox` (about line 2104; used by the comparison at about line 1561) changes to:
     - True: `true`, `yes`, `y`, `1`, `x`, `checked`, `on`, `t` (case-insensitive, trimmed).
     - False: `false`, `no`, `n`, `0`, `off`, `f`.
     - Blank: no change to the field.
     - Anything else: skip that field on that row and add a run-log note naming the column, row and value; the rest of the row still loads.
     - Order: admin guide import page first, then code; tests cover every accepted spelling, blank, an unrecognized value, and a 200-row bulk file.
  3. **Fix the dynamic Apex convention violations** (the conventions are in `docs/contributor-guide/dynamic-apex.md`, ADR-0013, ADR-0057, ADR-0058).
     - Bug: `HealthCheckOrgShapeChecks` (`CONNECT_MARKER_CLASS = 'ConnectService'`, line 18; renamed Mirror, D-103, so now `MirrorService` or its successor) looks for a class that Mirror does not ship, so Health Check always reports Mirror as not installed. Point it at a class Mirror does ship (namespace first, then bare), add a test through the seam, and correct item 4 of `packages/core/integration/c-07-c-11.permissions.md`, which still says Mirror must ship `ConnectService` (now `MirrorService`).
     - Uncapped counts: `ReceiptGapSelector` (on `main`; C-29 replaces it with `ReceiptGenerationFailureSelector`, check that one instead once C-29 merges), `CampaignSyncSelector.countAppeals`, and `HealthCheckSettingsChecks.errorLogFinding` (switch to the existing `countNewUpTo`).
     - Describes inside loops: `ImportMatcher.firstNameOf`, `ImportUndoBatch.fieldMap`, and `SettingsService.hasKey` with `SettingsController.toSetting`. Hoist each describe out of the loop and cache it per transaction.
     - Extension classes of the wrong shape: `ImportEntityProcessors` and `SampleDataModules` silently ignore a class found by `Type.forName` that does not implement the expected interface; log it to the error log instead. `SampleDataModules` should also cache its lookups.
     - CI gaps: add a static check that fails on Person Account field references (`IsPersonAccount`, `PersonEmail`, `__pc` fields) outside the allowed seams; extend `scripts/ci/check-standard-objects.sh` to cover the object and field declarations inside `packages/mirror`, not only Core and Giving. (The permission-set description length check was added with X-01.)
     - Tests: add a "Campaign unavailable" test seam so the Campaign-absent path is tested, not skipped; tests that return early when a feature is absent must assert both the present and the absent case.
  4. **Raise the sf CLI heap in the org test script.** Why: reading about 2,200 test results crashes the sf CLI with a JavaScript heap out-of-memory error, which the script then reports as a misleading "0 of 0 failed".
     - Export `NODE_OPTIONS=--max-old-space-size=8192` in `scripts/org/run-org-tests.sh`, and in `scripts/org/deploy-packages.sh` if a deploy shows the same crash.
     - Detect the out-of-memory signature in the CLI output and report "sf CLI crashed, rerun" rather than a test result, without posting a passing or failing `Org tests (local)` status.
     - Consider requesting a smaller result payload (for example omitting passing-test detail).
     - Update the heap paragraph in `docs/contributor-guide/ci.md`.
  5. **Smaller review follow-ups.**
     - X-01 Opportunity mirror: `OpportunityGiftService.isLinkDuplicate` checks the `DUPLICATE_VALUE` status code, but when the error names no fields it falls back to `error.getMessage().contains(<Opportunity_Id__c field name>)`, which a future field with a similar name could trip. Tighten the fallback (exact field token, or treat a field-less duplicate as not a link duplicate) and test both paths.
     - X-01 admin guide: warn against listing `Master` in the Opportunity record-type filter. It resolves to the Master record type's Id, but Opportunities without a record type carry a null value, so the run matches nothing and logs no warning. Alternatively, make the batch log a Warning when `Master` is listed.
     - C-29: optionally assert `Limits.getQueryRows()` in the receipt-cap test so the cap is proved, not only the result.
  6. **Rename the packaged metadata from Open Impact to BarnCRM (first job after C-29 lands; Brandon, 2026-10-12).** Why: the product name is cleared (Section 8.3), and API names become permanent at the first package version, so they must change before then. Scope:
     - Labels users see: the app ("Open Impact"), "Open Impact Settings", "Manage Open Impact Settings", the "Open Impact Admin", "Open Impact Staff", "Open Impact Read Only" and "Open Impact Duplicate Review" permission sets, "Pause all Open Impact automation", and every custom label, help text, LWC string and email body that says Open Impact.
     - API names: the `Open_Impact_Giving` report and dashboard folders (and every report and dashboard inside them), the `Open_Impact_Acknowledgments` email folder and the `Open_Impact_Thank_You` template, plus any Apex, LWC, Flow or test reference to those names.
     - Leave untouched: `packages/core/vendor/apex-rollup` (vendored third-party code), and historical statements in release notes and ADRs that describe what Open Impact was called at the time.
     - Order: decide with Brandon whether the `Nonprofit_*` API names (Section 11.2 item 25) are renamed in the same pass, since both are pre-first-version renames. Update the canonical model and admin guide first; split into PRs under about 800 changed lines; run the static checks (including the label and permission-set checks) and one full org-test run on the top head.
     - About 975 mentions across 350 files were counted on 2026-10-12, most of them in `packages/core` and `packages/giving`; docs were renamed separately the same day (PR #182).
     - Also in scope, found by the docs rename: the package names in `sfdx-project.json` ("Open Impact Core", "Open Impact Giving", "Open Impact Connect", "Open Impact Volunteers", "Open Impact Programs", "Open Impact Funders") and every doc that quotes them (package READMEs, admin guide, release notes), the "Open Impact Giving" report folder label, the "Open Impact: thank you for your gift" email label, the `openimpact` namespace examples in `docs/contributor-guide/ci.md` and `scratch-orgs.md`, and the C-29 Hub label "Welcome to Open Impact" (`HubHome` labels; `docs/admin-guide/nonprofit-hub.md` already says "Welcome to BarnCRM").
     - Merge order that avoids conflicts: C-29 (#163 to #174) first, then the docs PRs #181 and #182 rebased onto it (C-29 also edits docs), then this code pass.
     - **Merged 2026-10-12** as #181 (squash `87a00cb9`, gated 2,273 of 2,273 on `oi-test`; PRs #184 to #200 closed as landed). Earlier status, built, not merged: branches `feature/barncrm-rename-base` (C-29 top with `main` merged in, plus #181 and #182 cherry-picked; 9 admin-guide conflicts resolved keeping C-29's text) and `feature/barncrm-rename-1` (docs, tooling, `sfdx-project.json` package names "BarnCRM Core" and so on, `package.json` name `barncrm`), `-2` (Core labels and metadata), `-3` (Core Apex strings and comments), `-4` (Giving and Connect). API names moved: `Open_Impact_Giving` report and dashboard folders to `BarnCRM_Giving`, `Open_Impact_Acknowledgments` to `BarnCRM_Acknowledgments`, `Open_Impact_Thank_You` to `BarnCRM_Thank_You`; scheduled job names to "BarnCRM ..."; the `Nonprofit_*` API names are renamed to BarnCRM names on `feature/barncrm-api-names-1` to `-3` (PRs #198 to #200, now in `main`), following Section 11.2 item 25 (resolved 2026-10-12). Static checks, offline compile, lint, prettier and Jest pass; the full org-test gate runs on `oi-pa`. Old names remain only in vendored apex-rollup, historical release notes and ADRs, and this plan's path. The plan and its repo mirror dropped the "until the package rename" wording on 2026-10-13.
- **Work items from the 2026-10-12 landing** (PRs #214 to #218, landed via #219; not scheduled into an iteration unless noted):

This should also clear the `mwdbdevsb` check-only failure caused by the vendored tests (they referenced Data Protection and Privacy objects that org lacks); not re-run there.

Test self-containment follow-ups, state on `main` 2026-10-14: done: `scripts/org/run-org-tests.sh` no longer assigns BarnCRM permission sets (the gate proves the suite self-contained), the old "same running user" paragraph in `docs/contributor-guide/ci.md` is gone and its citation reads ADR-0090. ### 11.4 Decisions the builder must escalate rather than make

Adding any package dependency; adding a standard-object reference outside Mirror; adding a required Setup step; changing the household membership abstraction; changing the versioning or namespace; anything touching receipt immutability or gift amount mutability.

---

### 11.5 Module notes recorded during Core v1.0

Findings and decisions about the modules (Giving, Mirror, Volunteers, Programs, Funders), recorded here so they can be referenced when work on each module starts. They are not presented to Brandon until then (D-84). Grouped by module.

**Giving**
- **Erase a person: receipt PDFs (from PR #284, D-52).** Receipt PDFs are kept as tax records and still show the donor's name and address. The Erase a person preview tells admins to delete them by hand when retention allows. Recommended default, taken as the default. Alternative: Giving deletes them.
- **Label descriptions.** Giving (66 labels) and Mirror (38 labels) label descriptions still need the placeholder sweep before `scripts/ci/check-labels.py` covers them.
- **`HouseholdSoftCreditRecompute` chain limit (2026-10-13).** It chains one Queueable per household, so it stops after 5 households in Developer Edition, trial and scratch orgs; inside another Queueable it can still take that job's one chained slot. Candidate fix: a batch like `SampleDataStageBatch`, or a ready event like `Rollup_Queue_Ready__e`.
- **`GiftLookupSelector.selectHouseholdsWithGifts` runs without sharing (2026-10-13, #377).** The semi-join returns IDs only (ADR-0021 precedent), so a user who cannot see a household's gifts still queues `HouseholdSoftCreditRecompute`. Confirm against the Giving design when Giving work starts.
- **Deleting the default fund (2026-10-14, scale test).** The fund named as the default in Giving settings can be deleted, and every new gift is then refused with "No default fund is set". Candidate fix: refuse deleting the configured default fund, or Health Check flags a deleted or inactive default.
- **Re-splitting an existing gift's allocations (2026-10-14, scale test).** The gift entry screen is the only supported way, because allocation totals are validated after each DML statement, so no other path can change a split in place. Candidate fix: decide a supported path when Giving work starts.
- **Report and dashboard folder rights, G-M1 Medium (2026-10-28 security review).** Giving_Admin carries ManageReportsInPubFolders and ManageDashbdsInPubFolders (plus folder-create rights), and Volunteers_Admin carries ManageReportsInPubFolders; these let the holder edit or delete every public report and dashboard. Candidate fix: drop them, keep RunReports, ViewPublic*, at most CreateCustomize* and EditMy*, and use folder sharing. **Fix in PR #719 (2026-10-29), owner approved 2026-10-29 (D-148), in landing batch 14:** the four rights are dropped from Giving_Admin and three from Volunteers_Admin, so a non-system-administrator admin no longer edits the shipped reports and dashboards in place (they save a copy, or a folder is shared with them as Manager).
- **Giving Lows, G-L1 to G-L5 (2026-10-28 security review).** G-L1: CSV formula injection in AcknowledgmentLetterMerge.escape (reuse the AccountingExportService FORMULA_STARTS guard). G-L2: AcknowledgmentWriter links the merge CSV (donor PII) with Visibility AllUsers unconditionally (try InternalUsers first, as ReceiptWriter does, ADR-0034). G-L3: StewardshipStartController.startPlan does not check the user can see the donor before the without-sharing insert (add a user-mode visibility read). G-L4: acknowledgment emails ignore HasOptedOutOfEmail (skip, or record a transactional-email ADR). G-L5: Giving_Admin has View All on Commitment__c but not Gift__c, with no reason (justify or remove). **G-L1, G-L2 and G-L3 fixed in PR #717 (2026-10-29).** G-L4 and G-L5 are left for an owner decision, because each changes what users get: G-L4 recommendation, honor the opt-out (an opted-out donor's email acknowledgment is not sent, and the queue says why, so staff can send a letter instead), with an ADR; the alternative is an ADR that treats acknowledgments as transactional email. G-L5 recommendation, remove View All on Commitment__c so it matches Gift__c (both Private, record access through roles); the alternative is to justify it in the description and keep it. **Owner decided 2026-10-29 (honor the opt-out; remove View All). G-L4 and G-L5 fixed in PR #722 (2026-10-29).** G-L4: an email gift whose recipient contact (the donor, or the account's primary contact for an organization or household gift) has HasOptedOutOfEmail is not emailed, gets no row and no error, stays To acknowledge, and the letter run takes it; the Acknowledgments page counts it on its own line, Opted out of email, sent by letter (ADR-0144, canonical model R-AK13; Contact Point consent not read). G-L5: Giving_Admin loses View All on Commitment__c; the nightly top up and overdue pass reach only what the scheduler can see, so they are scheduled as a system administrator.
- **Blank settings with no default shown (2026-10-29 v0.11 QA, F4).** Default appeal, Acknowledgments from address and Opportunity mirror start date (Date and Time) show an empty control with no Default badge, against the owner rule that every setting shows a changeable default. Receipts: Statement year and Place of issue, likewise. Core (PR #706) now shows "Not set" only for blank Text rows of Core settings and of the Organization page (so the tax ID and receipt signer, logo and signature rows are covered); each of these rows needs its own meaning stated by Giving (for example what a blank from address sends as, which year a blank statement year means). Candidate fix: a shipped default where one exists, else a stated meaning per row.
- **Template names not in Title Case (2026-10-29 v0.11 QA).** Shipped import template names such as "Generic gift list" read in sentence case where the owner rule wants Title Case for names shown as titles. Candidate fix: rename the template records' labels, keeping their developer names.
- **Sample data load stops partway at FundSelector (2026-10-29/30 QA v15 to v19, F2, minor).** A Standard User with BarnCRM Admin and Volunteers Admin but no Giving permission set loads Core records (226 accounts), then the Giving stage fails with `System.QueryException: sObject type 'Fund__c' is not supported` at `FundSelector.selectDefaultFund` (GivingSampleDataStages, stage 0), leaving a half-loaded set. Candidate fix: check Giving access before loading and either refuse with a message naming Giving Admin or skip the Giving stage with a note.
- **A Standard User can clone a report into the shipped Giving report folder (2026-10-29/30 QA v15 to v19, F4, minor, needs investigation).** With Create and Customize Reports from the profile (BarnCRM Staff plus Giving Staff, or Giving Admin), `POST /services/data/v62.0/analytics/reports?cloneId=<shipped Giving report>` with no folder, or with the BarnCRM Giving folder Id, returns 200 and saves the new report in the BarnCRM Giving folder. Editing or deleting the shipped reports there is correctly refused (403). Not checked whether the UI Save As also offers that folder. Candidate fix: confirm the folder's sharing (Viewer only for everyone) in an installed org; if the platform still allows it, document it or ship the folder with explicit view-only sharing.

**Mirror**
- Label descriptions: see the note above (38 labels).
- **No security findings (2026-10-28 security review).** The Inbound Gift endpoint's missing custom permission is already an accepted checklist item; explain it in the submission.
- **"Opportunity mirror record types" has no control (2026-10-29 v0.11 QA, F1, major).** The setting row renders empty: no picker of Opportunity record types and no default shown. Expected: a picker of Opportunity record types showing its default (for example "All record types"). Candidate fix: give the row a Record Type List definition with an all-types default, so the Core console renders it.

**Rollups**
- **Rollup definitions editable outside the gated Builder, R-H1 High (2026-10-28 security review).** Rollup_Builder_Admin grants Create, Edit and Delete on Rollup_Definition__c and edit on the target, source, filter and bypass fields, without Manage_BarnCRM_Settings. RollupDefinitionValidationHandler checks only type compatibility and the bypass permission; RollupPlanner.engineCanWrite accepts isUpdateable or isPermissionable; reads run SYSTEM_MODE without sharing and RollupWriter writes SYSTEM_MODE. So the nightly run can overwrite any permissionable field or copy a restricted field into a readable one. Candidate fix: enforce RollupBuilderController.checkedRecord's checks in the trigger handler on insert and on any change to calculation fields (permission, target isUpdateable, source, order and filter fields isAccessible), or remove Create and Edit on the object from the set and save only through the controller; add a target-object allowlist (at least exclude User and setup objects). **Fixed in PR #720 (2026-10-29):** RollupDefinitionGuard, called from the trigger itself, requires Manage BarnCRM Settings for any change but the engine's Last Calculated stamp, and on insert or a change to what a rollup reads or writes, an allowed target object (never User, setup objects or BarnCRM bookkeeping), an editable target field and readable read fields; only the shipped defaults are trusted.
- **Rollups Lows and Info (2026-10-28 security review).** R-I1: escapeSingleQuotes wraps whole describe-built query strings (RollupDlrs.cls:292, RollupDlrsImport.cls:574, 585, 597); drop it and cite describe. R-I2: RollupQueue.cls:382 deletes queue rows without an explicit AccessLevel; add SYSTEM_MODE. Also a severity 2 MissingFaultHandler in Recalculate_Rollups_Template.flow. **Fixed in PR #720 (2026-10-29):** R-I1 escaping dropped; R-I2 was a comment quoting a delete, reworded (every real DML already states its access level); the flow template has a fault path.
- **Rollup Builder wording and casing (2026-10-29/30 QA v15 to v19, F5, cosmetic).** Buttons are sentence case where the rest of BarnCRM uses Title Case: "Schedule nightly recalculation", "Recalculate all", "Restore shipped rollups", "New rollup", "Export to a file". "About 675 records, roughly 1 minutes." is not pluralized correctly. "Rollups Last Completed:Not calculated yet" has no space after the colon. The stale warning "Open the Rollups page to start them." shows on the Rollups page itself. Candidate fix: Title Case the buttons, fix the plural, add the space, reword the warning on this page.

**Volunteers**
- **Template names not in Title Case (2026-10-29 v0.11 QA).** Shipped import template names such as "Volunteer hours" read in sentence case; same fix as the Giving note above.
- **Shift Name on the New form (2026-10-25).** Shift Name stays required on the shift layout, because the platform requires Name on a layout, so the New form needs a name. Blank-name auto-naming works for the API, import and schedules. Candidate later: a quick action with a prefilled name.
- **Team Members cannot set a person's first Volunteer Status (2026-10-25, PR #550).** The Volunteer Profile section shows only once a status is set, and only Admin and Coordinator hold Manage Volunteers, so a Coordinator sets a new person's first status. A Team Member (Volunteers Staff) can edit the status afterwards. Candidate fix when Volunteers work resumes: a custom permission for setting the first status, or a visibility rule change.
- **Unexplained View All and Modify All, V-L1 Low (2026-10-28 security review).** Volunteers_Admin and Volunteers_Coordinator grant View All and Modify All on Skill__c, Volunteer_Skill__c and Volunteer_Requirement__c (Admin also Volunteer_Letter_Template__c) with no recorded reason; only Volunteer_Hours__c is explained. Candidate fix: ReadWrite sharing for reference objects instead of Modify All; justify Volunteer_Requirement__c in the description. **Fixed in PR #718 (2026-10-29)** by stating each grant's reason in the object descriptions and the canonical model; the grants stay, because Read Write sharing would still need Modify All to delete another person's row, and on Volunteer Skill would let every staff member edit every row.
- **Sample Data card breaks for a BarnCRM Admin without Volunteers Admin (2026-10-29/30 QA v15 to v19, F1, minor).** Settings, General, Sample Data is reachable with BarnCRM Admin alone, but the Volunteers sample data hooks then throw: the card says "The sample data status could not be read..." and Load Sample Data fails with the generic "Something went wrong. The details are in the Error Log". Error Log: `System.SecurityException: Access to entity 'Volunteer_Hours__c' denied` at `VolunteerSampleDataSelector.countSampleHours` (status) and `'Volunteer_Job__c' denied` at `VolunteerSampleDataSelector.hasSampleJobsOrSkills` (load). Clears once the module access sync grants Volunteers Admin (within the hourly check). Candidate fix: the Volunteers sample data hooks check object access and count 0 or skip, and `SampleDataModules` catches a module's failure so one module cannot break Core's card.

**Cross-Module**
- **Inner async jobs with no sharing keyword (2026-10-28 security review, found by the new Core-only rule in check-apex-access-modes.py, PR #654).** giving HouseholdSoftCreditRecompute.RecomputeFinalizer, rollups RollupAccessSync.Job, RollupQueue.Drain, RollupService.SurvivorRecalculation, volunteers VolunteersAccessSync.Job. Candidate fix: declare with sharing on each and widen the rule to every package. **Fixed in PR #720 (2026-10-29):** each job states its sharing (Drain without sharing, as RollupQueue), and check-apex-access-modes.py checks async jobs in every package.

## 12. Decision log (initial ADRs; copy each into `docs/architecture/decisions/`)

| ID | Decision | Alternatives rejected | Why |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-01 | Product name "Open Impact" (chosen 2026-09-06), namespace candidate `openimpact` deferred and subject to verification; fallback name OpenCause. **Name superseded 2026-09-23 (Brandon):** "Open Impact" is not available; BarnCRM is the tentative name, subject to the Section 8.3 checks; the namespace stays deferred (ADR-0046). **Name cleared 2026-10-12 (Brandon):** the product is BarnCRM; rename status in Section 8.3 | "Nonprofit Commons" (collides with Salesforce.org's Open Source Commons program), "Next Impact" (echoes Salesforce's "next generation of Nonprofit Cloud" language), "Barnraise" (strong metaphor, not self-explanatory), "OpenNPC" (confusable with the Salesforce product) | Self-explanatory, signals open source and purpose, no known product collision |
| D-02 | Second-generation managed packages with one shared namespace for the AppExchange listing; built and piloted as unlocked packages under the same namespace until then, following managed-package rules so the same source converts (amended 2026-09-23, Brandon, ADR-0046; production pilots move through the C-31 migration) | Unlocked packages only (no push upgrades, no AppExchange managed listing); 1GP (legacy tooling) | AppExchange listing, push upgrades, cross-package access under one namespace |
| D-03 | Core plus separate module packages for heavy domains; feature flags for minor behavior | Single package with hidden features | Principle 3: an unused module leaves no schema behind |
| D-04 | Custom `Gift__c` as the gift system of record; Opportunity and Gift Transaction as optional mirrors in Connect (renamed BarnCRM Mirror, D-103) | Opportunity as the gift (NPSP); Gift Transaction (NPC) | Platform-license compatibility (large cost win for target customer), nonprofit semantics, no Industries dependency; mirrors preserve ecosystem compatibility |
| D-05 | Household as Account record type with two membership modes behind one service | Custom Household object; Party Relationship Groups | Standard object benefits and NPSP compatibility, while junction mode handles Person Accounts and multi-household membership |
| D-06 | Settings storage: protected hierarchy custom settings for toggles, custom objects for structured lists, custom metadata for shipped defaults materialized on install | All custom metadata (asynchronous writes, poor admin editing); all custom settings (not reportable, not versioned) | Synchronous front-end edits, upgrade-safe defaults, reportable configuration |
| D-07 | `sf` CLI plus GitHub Actions; CumulusCI optional later | CumulusCI from the start | Lower contributor barrier; revisit if multi-package orchestration becomes painful |
| D-08 | License: Apache-2.0 (confirmed by Brandon 2026-09-22) | BSD-3-Clause (NPSP precedent) | Explicit patent grant protects adopters and contributors |
| D-09 | No Person Account, Industries, OmniStudio, Data Cloud, or Experience Cloud dependency anywhere | Building on NPC primitives | Principle 4; the target customer cannot afford or administer them |
| D-10 | Receipts are immutable; corrections void and reissue; refunds are negative linked gifts | Editable amounts | Audit integrity; tax receipt correctness |
| D-11 | Rollup engine: evaluate vendoring a proven MIT-licensed Apex rollup library before building. **Superseded by D-16 (2026-10-12, Brandon):** BarnCRM owns its rollup engine; the vendored library is replaced and removed | Build from scratch first | Principle 8; correctness risk is highest in rollups |
| D-12 | Trunk-based development, squash merges, `main` always releasable | Git flow | Simplicity for a small team with AI agents |
| D-13 | Data management scope and phasing (Brandon, 2026-09-23; ADR-0047): an in-org tool with NPSP Data Importer parity, a Jetstream-style find and bulk update, and the useful dataimporter.io capabilities, the most useful in v1. Everything runs in the org with no call to Salesforce's own APIs; queries are compiled from a document and run in user mode, with no typed SOQL in v1 (C-36 later); bulk update is a journaled data job on `Import_Batch__c`, undoable through the import journal; gift import and donation matching (G-23, G-24) move to v0.5; a new v0.7 Data management iteration holds C-32 to C-35; bulk delete (C-37) and scheduled imports and connections to other systems (C-38) come after 1.0. Never built: Jetstream's metadata, Apex, permission and API tools, and dataimporter.io's org-to-org migration and backup | Leave data management to Data Loader, Jetstream and dataimporter.io; a Bulk API path through a Named Credential; typed SOQL from v1; bulk update with no undo; reusing Jetstream's code; folding C-34 and C-35 into Hardening instead of a new iteration | Maria works in labels, not API names, and needs a preview and an undo; every read and write runs with her own access and sharing; no connected app, stored token or third party holds donor data; a Lightning session cannot call the REST or Bulk API; Jetstream is Apache-2.0 with the Commons Clause, not open source, so its ideas are used and its code is not; a new iteration keeps the three-major rule and puts the tools in front of the beta cohort |
| D-14 | Cancelled status for unpaid Pending gifts (Brandon, 2026-09-24; amends G-04): a Pending gift that will never be paid is set to Cancelled, a non-money step with no negative gift, excluded from rollups, the accounting export, receipts and retention reports, and not blocked by the G-20 posting flag or period lock; Written off is kept for gifts that were Received. Built on top of G-20 in v0.6; recorded in ADR-0053 (posted gifts and closed periods are locked) and ADR-0054 (a pending gift that will never be paid is cancelled), amending ADR-0022 and the write-off and refund ADRs | Writing off an unpaid Pending gift as a linked negative gift; deleting unpaid Pending gifts (the admin-guide advice it replaces) | A gift that was never paid was never in the books, so it needs no reversing entry and no posting or period-lock check; deleting it loses the record |
| D-15 | Second long-lived test org (Brandon, 2026-09-24): a scratch org with Person Accounts enabled, alias `oi-pa`, kept until it expires and recreated after that, used for Person Account testing and as a backup gate org. It is the v0.5 Person Account org shape, gated locally through `scripts/org/run-org-tests.sh` | A Person Account shape in a GitHub CI matrix; Person Account testing only in short-lived scratch orgs | Org tests run locally, not in GitHub CI; Person Account orgs behave differently in subtle ways (Section 11.1), and a second gate org keeps testing possible when the main test org is unavailable |
| D-16 | BarnCRM owns its rollup engine (Brandon, 2026-10-12; ADR-0082 supersedes ADR-0015 and amends ADR-0011 reuse-first for rollups; proposal decisions D1 to D9 with amendments A1 and A2). D2: definitions stay in `Rollup_Definition__c`, shipped defaults in `Rollup_Definition_Default__mdt`. D3: every affected total is recalculated from all its source records, never added to and subtracted from. D4: a normal save updates totals in the same save, a bulk load is queued and finishes within minutes, the nightly run stays as the safety net; modes are named Instantly, Every night, Both (recommended). D5: instant mode for BarnCRM's own objects through shipped triggers (admin objects as amended by A2). D6: smallest feature set first (Total, Count, Smallest, Largest, Average, First, Last; direct lookups plus household paths; filters; fiscal-year windows), deferred items listed with what brings them back. D7: a step-by-step wizard from standard Lightning base components, with a live preview on a real record and a Needs attention list with Retry. D8: register real time on the Giving objects and write the missing tests now; fix target filters and both freshness stamps inside the new engine. D9: the old engine is deleted only after a clean comparison run; the v0.11 scale test adds skew and parallel-load cases with pass targets (Section 7.3). A1: the Rollup Builder is a dedicated tab and app page where every rollup, shipped or admin-made, can be created and edited. A2: for an admin's own object the Builder generates a trigger plus its test class and deploys them through the Metadata API, chosen deliberately over handing the admin generated files; mitigations required (deploy prerequisites, a plain message for coverage failures, the known DLRS failure modes addressed in the ADR). **Amended 2026-10-26 (Brandon, D-130, D-132, ADR-0138):** A1's tab lives in the BarnCRM admin apps, with no separate Rollup Builder app; A2's one-click deploy is deferred: the Builder generates a one-line trigger and its test and the admin deploys them (each trigger has its own automation switch) | Keep apex-rollup and fix the wrapper (cheapest; fails the ownership goal, keeps about 25,000 vendored lines and local patches, delta math races under parallel loads); apex-rollup as a package dependency; a platform event queue; per-transaction Queueables without a queue; admin-made definitions as custom metadata; native roll-up summaries and Flow aggregation (insufficient); for admin objects, only a Flow template or a hand-written trigger (D5 as first written) | Owner's independence goal; most of the engine is already BarnCRM's and apex-rollup is reached at three call sites; recompute is correct by construction under concurrent work; the five rollup bugs found 2026-10-12 are fixed by construction in the new engine; admins get instant totals on their own objects without code |
| D-17 | Import gaps closed (Brandon, 2026-10-12: "proceed as recommended; I can amend later"): import matches by Salesforce record ID and offers a "match only, never create" option (resolves Section 11.2 item 15 as yes), and the import wizard accepts custom fields on person and organization rows, both in C-32; Quick Gift Entry and Gift Batches get a "pays this payment" picker in Giving, reusing G-24 matching | Leaving these gaps open until after 1.0 | Nonprofit Cloud and NPSP migrations load into orgs where the people already exist; admins add custom fields to people and organizations; staff entering a payment need to say which pledge payment it pays |
| D-18 | Rollup comparison recommendations adopted (Brandon, 2026-10-12; extends D-16, C-13): new slices S15 (Metadata API connection spike), S16 (generate and deploy trigger) and S17 (remove trigger, with a Health Check); an operations slice after S8 (count distinct, checkbox Any or None, First or Last into a text field, text lists); multi-currency behavior and a Builder warning in S5; a queue-drain target in the v0.11 scale gate; a time estimate shown before Recalculate all. Deferred: sandbox-to-production export, last N days windows, failure emails, grandchild, hierarchy and Task or Event paths | Building every compared feature before 1.0 | Closes the gaps that block common rollups and the A2 trigger path; the deferred items are rarer and each can return on demand |
| D-19 | Confidential interaction notes are not built (Brandon, 2026-10-12): interaction notes stay completed standard Tasks that follow record visibility (Section 11.3) | A confidential flag or a separate notes object with its own sharing | Keeps one sharing model that admins already understand |
| D-20 | A gift stays credited to the household it was credited to when given (Brandon, 2026-10-12; amends canonical model rule R-G2; ADR-0071 `0071-a-gift-keeps-the-household-it-was-given-to.md`): a donor moving households does not move past gifts. Household rollups reached through current membership (soft credits) follow current membership | Moving past gifts to the donor's new household | Household giving history, receipts and accounting exports stay as they were when the gift was given |
| D-21 | "What's new" release notes (C-27) are labelled with the release they ship in; the first is "0.6" until a version is set (Brandon, 2026-10-12) | Labels by date or by feature ID | Admins match a note to the version they installed |
| D-22 | ADRs accepted (Brandon, 2026-10-12; ADR-0088, ADR-0090, ADR-0072, ADR-0073, ADR-0089, ADR-0074, ADR-0075): unlocked-to-managed migration kit (ADR-0088); Flow templates (each automation ships a template or a documented reason it does not; ADR-0090); suite choice is the first setup step (ADR-0072); Module Manager (turning a module off stops its automation, cannot hide tabs, no version column; ADR-0073); interaction notes as completed Tasks (ADR-0089); record pickers in the settings console (ADR-0074); Health Check schedules every nightly job (ADR-0075) | Leaving them Proposed | Unblocks the builds that depend on them |
| D-23 | Platform capability recommendations adopted (Brandon, 2026-10-12): keep LWC (watch Multi-Framework; an ADR is needed before adoption); audit the API 67.0 default access mode (in progress); Apex Cursors for large-volume jobs (ADR to come with C-33 and C-35); GraphQL in new screens; AI-ready invocable action descriptions and an admin guide page (no shipped AI, no paid API); cheap portability steps (a canonical model completeness CI check, OpenAPI for the inbound gift API, a pure-JS lint rule). Avoid Data 360, Heroku AppLink, Functions, Lightning Types, Named Query API and Lightning Out 2.0 | Adopting the newer runtimes and paid services now | Principle 4: no paid add-ons or new dependencies for the target customer; cheap steps keep the product portable and AI-ready |
| D-24 | Telemetry (C-26) deferred indefinitely (Brandon, 2026-10-12: "There is no AppExchange for now so if telemetry is about package usage, that can be indefinitely deferred."). If an AppExchange listing happens later, prefer AppExchange App Analytics over building telemetry. Resolves open question 7 in Section 11.2 | Building opt-in anonymous telemetry in v0.11 | Telemetry measures package usage, which needs a listing; none is planned, so the work and its privacy disclosure have no payoff now |
| D-25 | CMDT Manager scope (ADR-0085; accepted under the owner's standing instruction 'proceed as recommended', 2026-10-12; owner may amend): only Relationship Type is editable, and only the org's own rows; shipped rows are read only; it is a section of BarnCRM Settings (General), not a tab; the BarnCRM Metadata Admin permission set carries no Customize Application or Modify Metadata | Every custom metadata type editable, as Section 6.1 item 9 read; moving relationship types to a custom object (still open) | A subscriber row in a materialized or registry type would never reach what the feature reads, or would name a class or field that does not exist; shipped rows stay upgrade-safe |
| D-26 | Field utilization ships in Core (ADR-0086; its schedule, batch, tracked-list and retention parts are superseded by D-37; accepted under the owner's standing instruction 'proceed as recommended', 2026-10-12; owner may amend): Core, not its own Analytics package; a weekly user-mode count on Sundays at 04:00; 13 months of snapshots; an object list of up to 255 characters; Account, Contact and custom objects only (other standard objects would belong in Connect later; renamed BarnCRM Mirror, D-103) | A separate Analytics package; system-mode counts; nightly runs kept forever | No install step or dependency for one object and one page; user mode is the narrowest read that answers the question; weekly runs and 13 months bound the storage |
| D-27 | Typed Find queries are parsed into the query document in the browser (C-36, ADR-0078; accepted under the owner's standing instruction 'proceed as recommended', 2026-10-12; owner may amend): the server never takes raw SOQL and checks the document exactly as for the visual builder | Accepting SOQL text on the server; parsing on the server | One enforcement path; no typed text reaches a query |
| D-28 | Column changes are a declarative transform document, and import can match through the org's duplicate rules (C-37 part 1, ADR-0081; accepted under the owner's standing instruction 'proceed as recommended', 2026-10-12; owner may amend) | A formula language evaluated in Apex; regular expressions in find and replace | Nothing an admin types is evaluated as code; admins reuse the duplicate rules they already keep |
| D-29 | Health Check keeps its orphan findings after the Data Hygiene tab (C-28) ships (accepted under the owner's standing instruction 'proceed as recommended', 2026-10-12; owner may amend) | Moving the orphan findings out of Health Check into the Data Hygiene tab | Health Check stays the one place that lists every problem; Data Hygiene is where they are fixed |
| D-30 | A nightly rollup run is skipped, and logged, when a Recalculate all is already running at 2 AM (rollup S8; accepted under the owner's standing instruction 'proceed as recommended', 2026-10-12; owner may amend) | Queuing the nightly run behind it; running both | One full run at a time; the running job already recalculates every total |
| D-31 | Rollup operations slice (accepted under the owner's standing instruction 'proceed as recommended', 2026-10-12; owner may amend): count distinct is its own operation; the text list delimiter is a picklist (comma, semicolon, new line); Any and None read no field; count distinct writes plain number fields only; a rollup whose target field cannot hold its result is refused with a plain message | Count distinct as an option on Count; a free-text delimiter | Plain choices an admin cannot get wrong; refusing a bad target beats a failing nightly run |
| D-32 | Rollup Builder (S10 to S13; accepted under the owner's standing instruction 'proceed as recommended', 2026-10-12; owner may amend): the Recalculate all estimate uses 1,650 records a minute until the scale test measures it; S14 is built only if the scale test needs it | Waiting for a measured rate before showing an estimate | An approximate estimate now is more useful than none; the rate is a constant to update |
| D-33 | Tests run as a permission-set user (accepted under the owner's standing instruction 'proceed as recommended', 2026-10-12; owner may amend): `TestUsers.admin()` (and `adminWith(...)`) creates a user on the running user's profile with the BarnCRM permission sets; `scripts/ci/check-tests-run-as.py` checks every test method, with 12 recorded opt-outs | Standard User profile for test users; relying on the gate assigning permission sets to the deploying user | A Standard User would change Private-record visibility and user-mode user inserts; tests must pass in any org a check-only deploy reaches |
| D-34 | C-37 part 2 choices (ADR-0091; accepted under the owner's standing instruction 'proceed as recommended', 2026-10-13; owner may amend): the bulk delete run limit defaults to 10,000 and is at most 48,000; `Bulk_Delete_Records` ships only in its own `BarnCRM_Bulk_Delete` permission set, not in BarnCRM Admin or the role groups, and is assigned together with Find and Export; undo lasts until the earlier of the import undo window and 15 days; a transform result the field cannot take is refused for that record in bulk update, while import truncates it | A larger default run limit; Bulk_Delete_Records inside BarnCRM Admin; undo for the whole import undo window; truncating in bulk update as import does | Deleting is the most destructive tool, so it is opt-in by permission and bounded by default; undelete works only while records are in the recycle bin (15 days); a refused record is visible, a silently shortened value in an existing record is not |
| D-35 | Merge survivors' real-time totals are recalculated by a queueable just after the merge (ADR-0092; accepted under the owner's standing instruction 'proceed as recommended', 2026-10-13; owner may amend) | Recalculating inside the merge itself; leaving the totals for the next scheduled run | Totals are right moments after a merge without adding rollup work to the merge transaction |
| D-36 | Import chooses a Person Account record type available to the running user; if there is none it creates nobody and logs one note (#221; accepted under the owner's standing instruction 'proceed as recommended', 2026-10-13; owner may amend) | Using a record type the user cannot use and failing every row | One clear note beats a flood of identical row errors, and no record is created under a record type the user cannot see |
| D-37 | Field utilization runs on demand from its own Field Utilization tab (owner decision, Brandon, 2026-10-13; a new ADR on `feature/field-utilization-on-demand`, numbered when it lands, records it): pick an object, and its fields and their utilization are shown; there is no nightly or weekly job. Supersedes the schedule, batch, tracked-list and retention parts of ADR-0086 and the weekly run in D-26. `FieldUtilizationJob` is not registered in `NightlyJobs` | The weekly user-mode snapshot job with 13 months of history (D-26) | One question asked on demand needs no scheduled job, stored history or tracked-object list |
| D-38 | Priority (owner decision, Brandon, 2026-10-13): existing Core features are thoroughly tested, optimized, improved and simplified, without removing functionality, before work moves to scheduled imports and connections (C-38) or other non-core modules (Volunteers, Programs, Funders, X-07 and so on). A Core quality pass (review under way) comes first and C-38 stays after it (Section 6.0) | Continuing with new modules and C-38 while the Core review runs | Core is what every other module stands on; defects and complexity found now cost less than after more modules depend on it |
| D-39 | Platform features in the Core quality pass, and C-38 on hold (owner decision, Brandon, 2026-10-13; extends D-38 and D-23): all 14 recommended platform features are part of the Core quality pass and are built in parallel now as Q-01 to Q-14 (Section 6.0), each on its own branch `feature/q-NN-<slug>`; Q-14 (Agentforce-ready invocable actions) is optional and approved. Scheduled imports and connections (C-38) stay on hold until Brandon is ready to start the next version, which is stronger than D-38's "after the Core quality pass" | Adopting the features one at a time after the pass; starting C-38 as soon as the pass ends | Standard platform features replace custom code and speed up the gates while Core is already being tested and simplified; the next version's start stays an owner call |
| D-40 | Retention and limits (owner decision, Brandon, 2026-10-13, as recommended): Error Log entries older than 180 days are deleted; staged import rows are purged 90 days after their job finishes (amends Section 4.9, which purged them on commit); both periods are adjustable in BarnCRM Settings; every number setting has a minimum and a maximum | Keeping error entries and staged rows until an admin deletes them; purging staged rows on commit; number settings without bounds | Bounded storage for a small nonprofit without losing recent history; a bounded setting cannot be saved with a value that breaks a job |
| D-41 | Import scope (owner decision, Brandon, 2026-10-13, as recommended; **the affiliation part is reversed by D-49**, the same day): affiliations stay out of Core imports, and the spec and admin guide are corrected to match (amends C-32 and Section 4.9); import templates shipped in a later version are added to existing orgs automatically on upgrade, not only through Health Check | Resolving affiliations in Core imports; adding new templates only through Health Check | Keeps Core import to organizations, households and people; admins get new templates without a manual step |
| D-42 | "What's new" entries (C-27) are refreshed from the release notes as part of each release (owner decision, Brandon, 2026-10-13, as recommended) | Writing the entries by hand separately | One source for what changed; the in-app notes cannot drift from the release notes |
| D-43 | The 9 components used only inside the BarnCRM Settings console are no longer offered in App Builder (owner decision, Brandon, 2026-10-13, as recommended) | Leaving them exposed | An admin cannot place a component on a page where it does not work |
| D-44 | The Fundraising Staff permission set group includes Giving Staff (owner decision, Brandon, 2026-10-13, as recommended); closes Section 11.2 item 12 | Removing the group before the first package version | The group then grants what fundraising staff need, so it earns its place in the package |
| D-45 | The renamed import tab reads "Data Jobs" in Title Case (owner decision, Brandon, 2026-10-13, as recommended; **the tab label is amended to "Import" by D-51**, the job object label stays "Data Job"); amends ADR-0047's "Data jobs" wording | Sentence case "Data jobs" | Matches the Title Case of the other BarnCRM tab names |
| D-46 | A Dev Hub org snapshot `BarnPA` is taken from `oi-pa` (owner approval, Brandon, 2026-10-13; Q-12) | Recreating the Person Account org from scratch each time | A Person Account test org can be recreated quickly when `oi-pa` expires |
| D-47 | Finish Core before version 1 (owner decision, Brandon, 2026-10-13): duplicate rule matching and anything else unfinished in Core is finished before version 1, excluding the post-1.0 themes (Section 6.1) and C-38; the inventory of unfinished Core work is done (2026-10-14, #401 to #408; Section 6.0) | Shipping version 1 with partial Core features | Core is what every module stands on; version 1 should not ship with half-built Core features |
| D-48 | Core review recommendations approved (owner decision, Brandon, 2026-10-13): rollups recalculate only the definitions that marked a target, which needs an ADR and narrows ADR-0082 decision 2; Instantly-mode fiscal-window rollups are included in the nightly run; Recalculate now checks the budget and queues; an import commit runs the previewed template; organizations are created only for rows that pass; a people import reads values the way Load one object does; bulk delete undo covers deleted empty households; Health Check on Home shows the last result with a Run now button; one shared LWC helper module; faster test runs (fewer deploy stages, test users created once per class); namespace-safe object resolution. Code coverage is still checked automatically on every gate (not opt-in): read through `ApexCodeCoverageAggregate` after the run | Leaving each as found; making coverage opt-in | Fixes real defects and cost found in the Core reviews without removing functionality; coverage stays a standing gate |
| D-49 | Affiliations return to Core imports (owner decision, Brandon, 2026-10-13): a people import links each person to the organization on their row by an affiliation, as originally planned. **Reverses the affiliation part of D-41**; Section 4.9 and the C-32 row are restored accordingly | Keeping affiliations out of Core imports (D-41) | A person loaded with an employer should arrive linked to it; one row then describes the whole relationship as the plan first intended |
| D-50 | Data tools parity (owner decision, Brandon, 2026-10-13, from the dataimporter.io comparison; D-13 still holds: in-org, no org-to-org migration or backup). Build: a **Data Manager** hub with one job history and Undo; an **Org Optimizer** page (Data Hygiene relabeled; sections Overview, Data quality, Duplicates moved from Settings with a link left behind, Field utilization embedded only here and not in the navigation, Health Check on demand, Errors, Storage and limits, Unused setup admin-only, Automation and jobs); a full results file; a trial import (default 25 rows, a setting, changeable per file, then the remaining rows continue in the same job and the same undo); delete from a file of IDs; more transform steps; lookups and update matching on any Text, Email or Phone field; skip rows and one row per key; empty cells clear the field (off by default, journaled); delimiter and worksheet choice; download before delete; retry rejects into the same job; server-side export (the C-36 remainder, now in v1); parent and child loads. Not approved: seeding templates, files import. Scheduling and connectors stay C-38, on hold (D-39) | Leaving the data tools split across tabs; seeding templates; files import; building scheduling or connectors now | One place to start any data task and see what changed and undo it; closes the real gaps against dataimporter.io that fit Core |
| D-51 | The import tab label is "Import" (owner decision, Brandon, 2026-10-13; amends D-45 for the tab), reached from the Data Manager tab. Import and Bulk Update leave the app navigation; Find stays (amended by D-135: Find moves to Data Manager too). The job object label stays "Data Job" | Keeping "Data Jobs" on the tab; keeping Import and Bulk Update in the navigation | The Data Manager hub is now the data jobs place, so the tool itself reads as what it does |
| D-52 | Erase a person (right to be forgotten) is built in Core, with a Giving extension for gifts and receipts (owner decision, Brandon, 2026-10-13) | Leaving it out | Donors can ask for erasure under privacy laws; Core cannot reference Giving, so gifts and receipts are handled through an extension point |
| D-53 | The Commitment page keeps Clone and Printable View off (owner decision, Brandon, 2026-10-13) | Turning them on | A cloned or printed commitment risks duplicate pledges and stale payment schedules |
| D-54 | The Team Member (`Program_Staff`) and Coordinator (`Volunteer_Coordinator`) permission set groups get their module permission sets when those modules ship (owner decision, Brandon, 2026-10-13); until then they stay as they are | Removing the groups now | The groups are the place those module permissions will attach |
| D-55 | Shipped import templates are added at install and on upgrade (owner decision, Brandon, 2026-10-13); closes Section 11.2 item 14 | Copying them at first use or from Health Check only | Matches Section 4; admins get new templates without a manual step |
| D-56 | Org gates may batch several PRs into one combined org run and deploy only changed metadata; every test that would have run still runs (owner decision, Brandon, 2026-10-13). **Extended 2026-10-26 (Brandon, D-131):** combined landing batches with one full gate per batch; a light gate for screen, label and docs-only changes | One full gate per PR | Fewer deploys and runs with no test skipped |
| D-57 | Console app rollout (owner decision, Brandon, 2026-10-13): on the test orgs `oi-test`, `oi-pa` and `barn-core` Claude deletes and redeploys the app; on `barn-npc` and `mwdbdevsb` the owner switches Navigation Style to Console in App Manager | Deploying over the existing app everywhere | A deployed app cannot change its navigation type in place; the real-org sandboxes stay under the owner's hand |
| D-58 | Logo: the blue barn icon is approved as is (owner decision, Brandon, 2026-10-13; replaces the earlier red #B3261E): barn walls #00A8D9 (the blue in the Blue Bottle Coffee logo file), roof and app header #007A9E (same hue, 4.91:1 against white so header text stays readable); set on `feature/console-app-icon` at `4047fc85` | Other marks and colors; red | Approved by the owner |
| D-59 | Console tab icons: keep the page icons set by Q-07 (owner decision, Brandon, 2026-10-13) | Separate console tab icons | One icon set, no duplicate work |
| D-60 | Q-11: no Platform Cache for now; revisit after security review (owner decision, Brandon, 2026-10-13) | Adopting Platform Cache in the quality pass | Avoids cache of donor data and its security-review cost until the review is done |
| D-61 | Q-09: User Access Policies are documented with samples, not packaged (owner decision, Brandon, 2026-10-13) | Packaging policies | Policies are org-specific; samples let an admin adapt them |

Q-10 In-App Guidance tips are turned off in Setup under this rule | Leaving Setup steps in documentation only | An admin sees the step where the feature is configured |
| D-63 | Field utilization on demand: no 13-month auto-cleanup and no Health Check entry for the old job (owner decision, Brandon, 2026-10-13; amends D-37 follow-ups) | Auto-cleanup and a Health Check entry | The old scheduled job is gone; nothing remains to check or clean |
| D-64 | ADR-0088 (unlocked-to-managed migration kit) and ADR-0091 (bulk delete from a Find query with undelete) are accepted; rollup S16 and S17 are deferred to after version 1; the per-automation switch end date stays; neutral labels and one app stay (owner decisions, Brandon, 2026-10-13). **Amended 2026-10-26 (Brandon, D-124):** S16 and S17 start now, as the admin-deployed one-line trigger (ADR-0138, D-132), so one-click Metadata API deploy is no longer "planned after v1" but deferred until a spike proves a connection without a session id in a callout or per-org connected app setup; **amended by D-130:** four apps replace the one app | Dropping the end date; per-module apps or labels | Closes the open builder decisions |
| D-65 | The coverage gate measures BarnCRM classes at 75% in total and per class, not org-wide; the 23 classes now below 75% are held at their current figures until tests lift them (owner decision, Brandon, 2026-10-13; refines D-48's standing coverage check) | An org-wide figure | Other packages' code in the org cannot hide or sink BarnCRM's coverage; no class may fall further |
| D-66 | Q-05 wording and order (owner decision, Brandon, 2026-10-13): confirm buttons in standard order (Cancel, then the action); message-only toasts show the text as the bold main line; the Health Check finding title is not bold in the confirm | Action first; plain-weight toast text | Matches Lightning conventions |
| D-67 | Q-04: job finish notifications are on by default (owner decision, Brandon, 2026-10-13) | Off by default | Admins learn when a long job ends without finding the setting first |
| D-68 | The four Q-14 agent-ready actions get their access through packaged permission sets and/or permission set groups, not a manual Setup step (owner decision, Brandon, 2026-10-13) | A manual Setup step to grant access | Follows D-62: avoid Setup changes; access ships with the package |
| D-69 | Q-14 scope accepted (owner decision, Brandon, 2026-10-13): the four action classes are global (permanent once managed); no Update Person action, since the standard Update Record action covers it; starting or committing imports from an assistant is out of scope | An Update Person action; import actions for assistants | A small, permanent surface; imports keep their preview and typed confirmation in the app |
| D-70 | Record cards (Addresses, Affiliations, Relationships) that refuse a change for lack of access show a friendly message and write no Error Log entry; real failures are still logged (owner decision, Brandon, 2026-10-13; Q-06) | Logging every refused change | A permission refusal is expected behavior, not an error; the Error Log keeps real failures visible |
| D-71 | Rollup totals are recalculated just after the save by default (Background), with a "During the save" setting (Q-01, PR #256; owner decision, Brandon, 2026-10-13, as recommended); modifies ADR-0082 decision 4 through a new ADR written on that branch (number assigned at merge) | Recalculating during the save by default | The save is not slowed by recalculation; admins who need immediate totals can choose it |
| D-72 | `RollupRecalcAction` is global so a subscriber's Flow Builder can use it (owner decision, Brandon, 2026-10-13, as recommended); permanent once managed | Keeping it public | A subscriber's flow cannot call a non-global action |
