| D-76 | All BarnCRM triggers declare all seven contexts, with a CI check, after PR #229 merges (PLATFORM-18; owner decision, Brandon, 2026-10-13, as recommended) | Declaring only the contexts used | Uniform trigger declarations, enforced |
| D-77 | The unused label `Giving_SoftCredit_Duplicate` is deleted as a leftover (owner decision, Brandon, 2026-10-13, as recommended) | Keeping it | Nothing references it |
| D-78 | `Error_Log__c` sharing stays Read/Write (owner decision, Brandon, 2026-10-13, as recommended): every permission set has View All, only Admin can edit | Read only sharing | Read would break Mark as seen on other people's entries |
| D-79 | Stored household names use the org default language for joining words ("and", the list separator, "the late"), not the saving user's language (owner decision, Brandon, 2026-10-13, as recommended) | The saving user's language | A household name is stored data; it should not change with whoever saves it |
| D-80 | Plan corrections applied (owner decision, Brandon, 2026-10-13, as recommended; closes Section 11.2 item 27): the v0.3 row no longer lists NPSP import templates (they ship in v0.5); CI runs on one test org, not four org shapes; `TestDataFactory` is exposed with `@NamespaceAccessible` (ADR-0017), not `@IsTest global`; X-07 is v0.6 in both places that said v0.10; D-14 cites ADR-0053 and ADR-0054; the C-25 note says built, in `main`; Section 11.3 item 6 (the advanced find release note) is done as `v0.7-draft-advanced-find.md`; the v0.11 row says accessibility, mobile and translation are built, not merged (PRs #273 to #276), with the device mobile run still open | Leaving the plan stale | The plan matches the code and the other decisions |
| D-81 | Suites and packaging (owner decision, Brandon, 2026-09-23; numbered 2026-10-13): BarnCRM ships as two suites from the same packages, the Impact Suite (Core plus Giving, with Programs, Logic Models, Volunteers, Funders and Connect as modules; Connect renamed BarnCRM Mirror, D-103) and the Community Suite (Core alone, with the fitting modules); one install to start, guided installs after; Core is neutral; unlocked packages now, managed at listing; minor features are feature-flagged inside a package. The suite names here are superseded by D-96 (Impact Suite is now the Nonprofit Edition, Community Suite the Standard Edition). Full text in Section 4.1 | One product for all organizations; a base package that Core depends on | An unused module leaves nothing in the org (Principle 3) |
| D-82 | D-75 is reversed (owner decision, Brandon, 2026-10-13): the Bulk Update and Bulk Delete permission sets keep their own data job read grants | Moving the shared grants into BarnCRM Find and Export | Find and Export holders would then read every data job and the undo journal, which holds old field values |
| D-83 | Connect's (renamed BarnCRM Mirror, D-103) Record Inbound Gift action gets its own small permission set granting only that action (owner decision, Brandon, 2026-10-13); amends D-73, which named Giving's sets | Granting it through a broader set | Least access: only the action, nothing else |
| D-84 | Working agreement (Brandon, 2026-10-13): present only Core findings and decisions until he starts work on the modules; module feedback and decisions are recorded in Section 11.5 (grouped by module) so they can be referenced when those modules start; the goal now is Core v1.0 complete. Verbatim text in Section 9.6 | Presenting module findings alongside Core | Keeps attention on finishing Core v1.0 without losing module notes |
| D-85 | Working agreement (Brandon, 2026-10-13): implement recommendations and tell him what is being changed, unless it is really important or contrary to something he asked for or instructed. Clarification the same day: reviews or audits already in flight that relate to modules can finish, but their findings are not processed with him now unless they relate to Core. Verbatim text in Section 9.6 | Presenting every recommendation for approval | Fewer round trips; he is told what changed and still decides what is important or contrary to his instructions |
| D-86 | Erase a person (owner decision, Brandon, 2026-10-13; refines D-52): the preview lists activities and files for manual deletion, which are not erased automatically; Health Check skips erased people; PR #282 is accepted at its size | Erasing activities and files automatically; keeping erased people in Health Check findings | Activities and files may need to be kept or can hold other people's data, so an admin decides; an erased person is not a finding to fix |
| D-87 | Release prerequisites come after piloting v1.0 (Brandon, 2026-10-13): namespace registration, the Dev Hub and the steward entity (11.2 items 1, 3, 4) are settled after a v1.0 pilot; until then no package version is created, and upgrade-path tests wait for the first version | Settling them before v1.0 | Pilot evidence first; they block only package versions, not development or the pilot |
| D-88 | Deleting people never deletes their household (Brandon informed, 2026-10-13; canonical rule R-H12, landed in #377): a household emptied by deleting its people is kept in every membership mode; moving people still tidies it. Open option, tidy later after the Recycle Bin window: decided by D-89 (2026-10-14), landed in #408 | Deleting the emptied household in the same save | Deleting a parent purges children deleted before it from the Recycle Bin (confirmed on `oi-test`), so the deleted people could not be restored |
| D-89 | A household left with no members after its people are deleted moves to the Recycle Bin, but only after its former members can no longer be restored from the Recycle Bin (Brandon, 2026-10-14): a nightly Core job, on by default, switchable in Nonprofit Settings; households with other child records are kept and reported. Amends D-88, which protects restoring deleted people. Landed 2026-10-14 (#408, ADR-0120) | Deleting the emptied household in the same save (D-88 rejected it); never tidying empty households | The household is deleted only once restoring its people is no longer possible, so D-88's protection holds; the job is switchable, and households holding other child records are kept and reported rather than deleted |
| D-90 | 25,000 rows is the scale bar for the pilot (Brandon, 2026-10-14): the 250,000-row import run comes later, in a sandbox (Section 7.3) | Running the 250k-row import before the pilot | The 25k tier passed on `oi-test`; a 250k run needs a sandbox, and the pilot does not need it |
| D-91 | Salesforce-license-only permissions move out of BarnCRM Admin into the optional `BarnCRM_Access_Manager` permission set (ADR-0121, #406): Assign Permission Sets and View All Users; it has no role, `Duplicate_Review` stays Sales/Service-only, and the Access page checks up front and disables role and add-on changes without it | Keeping those permissions in BarnCRM Admin | Core must install and run on a Platform-only license (ADR-0013); a Platform-license user cannot hold those permissions |
| D-92 | Product-branded names stay (Brandon, 2026-10-14; closes Section 11.2 item 24): BarnCRM Admin, Staff, Read Only, Duplicate Review and BarnCRM Settings. Superseded for the Core roles by D-97. | Neutral Core names | The names are the product's; no reason to split labels between Core and Giving |
| D-93 | No end date on per-automation switches (Brandon, 2026-10-14; closes Section 11.2 item 16): they stay off until an admin turns them back on; the org-wide pause still expires (ADR-0055) | An optional end date per switch | Extra setting and job for little gain |
| D-94 | The pilot is a milestone below v1.0 (Brandon, 2026-10-14): no version number is attached to it; Brandon decides version numbers | Naming the pilot as a version | Version numbers are the owner's call; D-87 already settles release prerequisites after a pilot |
| D-95 | BarnCRM is open to any Salesforce org regardless of size, and extra valuable for small to mid-sized teams that lack the resources for many licenses and customization; it comes with powerful tools preloaded. The pilot is likewise open to any org size (Brandon, 2026-10-14). The Nonprofit Edition is described as "like NPSP for Nonprofit Cloud", plus other useful tools | Limiting the audience or the pilot to small teams (one to ten users) | Size is not the line; the value is highest where licenses and customization are scarce, and a pilot on varied org sizes gives better evidence |
| D-96 | Edition names (Brandon, 2026-10-14): "BarnCRM is the suite of tools that come in two versions": the **Nonprofit Edition** (formerly "Impact Suite") and the **Standard Edition** (formerly "Community Suite", for any other Salesforce org). Both editions have the full core suite of tools (people, households, relationships, addresses, imports with undo, Find and bulk update, duplicate review, Org Optimizer, Health Check, settings and the rest of Core); the Nonprofit Edition is the Standard Edition plus the nonprofit tools (Giving and the other nonprofit modules). The Standard Edition is never described as reduced, basic or a subset. "Nonprofit Edition" already named the Setup Assistant's toggle (D-81, ADR-0072) and the Impact Suite was described as that same thing (D-95), so there is now one meaning: the Setup Assistant's first step chooses between the two editions. Supersedes the suite names in D-81 and ADR-0046; D-81 is kept as written | "Community Edition" (clashes with Salesforce Community Cloud and the Trailblazer Community, and in software usually means a cut-down free tier); keeping "Impact Suite" and "Community Suite" (two names for the same offering, and "Community" misleads) | One name per offering; the Standard Edition is a full product, so no edition reads as a lesser one |
| D-97 | Core roles have neutral names (Brandon, 2026-10-14; supersedes D-92 for the Core roles): Program Staff becomes **Team Member** and Volunteer Coordinator becomes **Coordinator**, labels and descriptions only; API names `Program_Staff` and `Volunteer_Coordinator` unchanged; BarnCRM Admin, Read Only and Giving's Fundraising Staff unchanged (ADR neutral-role-names, PR #413) | Keeping the nonprofit role names (D-92) | Core is the whole Standard Edition (D-96); the names must fit any organization |
| D-98 | Advanced Territory Management (C-40) and a Grid tool (C-41) are planned Core Super Tools, built after v1.0 (Brandon, 2026-10-14). **Amended 2026-10-26 (Brandon, D-120):** C-40 becomes the optional module BarnCRM Territory Management, not Core; C-41 is unchanged | Not planned | Owner request; common paid add-ons small teams cannot afford |
| D-99 | Edition gates what Core shows (Brandon, 2026-10-14): nonprofit modules (Giving, Volunteers, Programs, Funders, Connect; renamed BarnCRM Mirror, D-103) are shown and offered only in the Nonprofit Edition; installed modules keep working; the module-to-edition mapping is data-driven. The edition is chosen once (first-run setup); afterwards it is changed only from BarnCRM Settings > General > Change edition (with confirmation) or Setup > Custom Settings, documented in the admin guide editions page. Volunteers moves from both editions to the Nonprofit Edition. Amends ADR-0072 and ADR-0062 decision 4 (ADR edition-gates-visibility, PRs #414, #415) | Edition hides nothing and can be changed any time in the Setup Assistant | Standard Edition orgs should not see nonprofit tools; edition is a one-time choice |
| D-100 | First-run setup and switches (Brandon, 2026-10-14): on first install, a setup overlay covers BarnCRM Home and BarnCRM Settings until an admin completes it (edition, then "turn on all defaults" or choose); non-admins see that an administrator must finish setup; it ends on Settings > General. General shows the edition and a panel of modules and features with on/off switches; when a feature or module is off, its app tabs and its Settings menu are hidden (tab visibility through permissions) | Setup Assistant step only; tabs left visible | Owner request for a clear first run and an uncluttered app |
| D-101 | Modules versus features (Brandon, 2026-10-14): features are installed with Core and can be switched on or off (Data Manager with Import, Find and Bulk Update; Duplicate Review; Org Optimizer and Health Check). Modules are separate packages not installed with Core, installed from BarnCRM Settings, shown only for their editions, each with its own console app and its own Settings tab, and each named "BarnCRM <name>" (BarnCRM Giving, BarnCRM Volunteers, BarnCRM Programs, BarnCRM Funders, BarnCRM Connect (renamed BarnCRM Mirror, D-103), BarnCRM Rollup Builder). "Super Tools" is a website and marketing name only. The Rollup Builder and rollup engine move out of Core into a new both-editions module, BarnCRM Rollup Builder, so orgs using DLRS or Rollup Helper can run them alongside BarnCRM; BarnCRM Giving depends on it (ADR-0125, rollup-builder-module). Landed 2026-10-15 in #438 (squash 4ed86f12). **Amended 2026-10-26 (Brandon, D-130):** the Rollup Builder module has no console app of its own; Rollup Builder lives in the BarnCRM admin apps | Keep rollups in Core behind a switch | Install-time choice for orgs with an existing rollup tool; Core stays smaller |
| D-102 | Features off by default, modules installed from Settings, first-run setup (Brandon, 2026-10-14 and 2026-10-15): Core features are off on a new install and are turned on only in first-run setup or later on Settings > General; turning one on is one-way, with a warning above the switches. Tabs and Settings menus stay hidden until their feature is on (packaged BarnCRM_Tab_* permission sets kept in step by a sync). Upgraded orgs count as every feature on. Modules are not installed with Core: they are installed only from the Settings > Modules section, which then shows "Installed" with a link to the module's app. A blocking first-run window on BarnCRM Home and BarnCRM Settings asks an admin for the edition, then "turn on all the default core features" or "let me choose" (all ticked); others see that an administrator needs to finish setup. A new install holds all Core automation, household creation included, until first-run setup turns features on; turning Household Upkeep on catches up people added before setup. Landed in #426 (ADR-0124) | Features on by default; modules bundled with Core | Admins choose what they use; nothing runs before it is chosen |
| D-103 | The module BarnCRM Connect is renamed **BarnCRM Mirror**, API names included (Brandon, 2026-10-15): package folder `packages/connect` becomes `packages/mirror`; `Connect_*` API names become `Mirror_*`. ADR-0126 (rename-connect-to-mirror) | Keep "Connect"; rename the label only | "Connect" reads as an integration product; the module is a mirror (Opportunity mirror, Campaign sync copies appeals, Gift Transaction mirror) plus an inbound gift API and accounting export |
| D-104 | v0.8 Volunteers started and its design approved (Brandon, 2026-10-17; ADR-0127 to ADR-0129): modules start now, ahead of plan 6.0 item 3. (1) Hours totals through the BarnCRM Rollup Builder module: Volunteers depends on Core and Rollup Builder and ships its own rollup definition defaults. (2) Hour letters and certificates use Volunteers' own small letter generator (the same HTML-to-PDF technique as receipts), not Giving's receipts engine, so Volunteers needs no Giving. (3) Volunteer fields live on the person (Contact and Account) and ship with Volunteers; onboarding steps are a child record (`Volunteer_Requirement__c`); skills come from an admin list with a junction. (4) Four permission sets (Admin, Coordinator with `Manage_Volunteers` to approve hours, Staff, Read Only), given to holders of the matching BarnCRM roles by an access sync. (5) Recurring shifts: a schedule record and a nightly job keep shifts created to a rolling horizon (default 8 weeks); edits change only future shifts with no sign-ups. (6) Hours approval is a status on the hours record (Submitted, Approved, Rejected), approved hours locked, only approved hours counted. (7) v0.8 is done when built and tested unmanaged; install-link testing waits for the first package versions. | Owner approved the recommended option for each; reuses the Rollup Builder module instead of a second engine; keeps Volunteers free of Giving; keeps background-check data off the person record |
| D-105 | Deleting a person removes that person's volunteer requirements and volunteer skills; merging two people keeps them (Volunteers V-05, integrator, 2026-10-18; taken as recommended by the integrator, owner may amend) | Keep the rows after a delete; delete them on merge too | Orphan requirement and skill rows have no use once the person is gone; a merge keeps the surviving person's history |
| D-106 | Volunteers ships its own copies of the Core person pages (the ADR-0064 pattern), so the Volunteer Profile, readiness card and letter action appear on the Volunteers app's person pages (integrator, 2026-10-18; taken as recommended by the integrator, owner may amend) | Edit the Core pages from Volunteers | Core stays neutral; the cost is that a copy can drift from Core's page, accepted as in ADR-0064 |
| D-107 | The Volunteers lapsing-volunteers report stays Contact-only, so it shows no rows in Person Account orgs (integrator, 2026-10-18; taken as recommended by the integrator, owner may amend) | A second report for Person Accounts | A report type has a single base object |
| D-108 | Erasing a person deletes the hour letters and certificates BarnCRM generated for them (identified exactly, deleted in system mode after the access check) and lists them as Deleted; every other File stays, listed by Core as Kept (#506, amends the 2026-10-20 entry) (integrator, 2026-10-20; taken as recommended by the integrator, owner may amend) | Leave the generated PDFs for an admin to delete, as with Giving receipts (the first 2026-10-20 entry) | Generated letters carry only the person's own data; every other File can be shared beyond the person, so it stays |
| D-109 | Removing sample data never deletes a real person's rows: sign-ups, hours and skills a real person holds on a sample shift or skill are kept, the sample job or skill stays, and removal stops with the reason in the Error Log (integrator, 2026-10-20; taken as recommended by the integrator, owner may amend) | Delete everything under the sample records | Sample removal must not destroy real data |

| D-111 | Contact Point sync is a new optional module, BarnCRM Contact Point Sync, not Core (Brandon, 2026-10-25; C-45): it keeps `ContactPointEmail`, `ContactPointPhone` and `ContactPointAddress` in step with the person's email and phone fields and `Address__c` for orgs that use Contact Points. A new ADR before build | Syncing Contact Points from Core | Core stays free of those standard objects (ADR-0110, ADR-0013, `check-standard-objects.sh`) |
| D-112 | Address verification is a new optional module, BarnCRM Address Verification (Brandon, 2026-10-25; C-46; amends Section 4.7, which put third-party verification out of scope): bring-your-own provider key set in the BarnCRM Settings console (never only in Setup); verifies `Address__c` and writes Verification Status and Latest Verified Date (the R-AD6 placeholder fields). No provider bundled; BarnCRM sells no paid service | Keep verification out of scope; bundle a provider | Verified addresses matter for donor mail; a bring-your-own key keeps BarnCRM free and self-service |
| D-113 | Related-record duplicate matching in Core, steps 1 to 3 (Brandon, 2026-10-25; C-44; Section 4.9): (1) configurable match sources in the BarnCRM Settings console (object, field, parent link to Contact or Account, normalization: email lowercase and trim, phone digits only, address normalized); (2) shipped source rows for `ContactPointEmail`, `ContactPointPhone`, `ContactPointAddress` and `Address__c`, active only when the object exists in the org (dynamic SOQL, as X-07 does); (3) the duplicate scan adds pairs found through these sources to the existing review list with a reason naming the source, and import matching uses the same sources | A save-time warning (explicitly not included) | Salesforce duplicate and matching rules cannot see related objects |
| D-114 | Erasing a person also empties Rejection Reason on their volunteer hours (Brandon, 2026-10-25; closes Section 11.2 item 28) | Keep it, listed by Core as Kept | A rejection reason can describe the person; erasure should leave nothing about them |
| D-115 | Erasing a person keeps deleting the hour letters and certificates BarnCRM generated for them, as D-108 already says; no behaviour change (Brandon, 2026-10-25; closes Section 11.2 item 29) | Keep the generated letters for an admin to delete | Generated letters carry only the person's own data, so erasure should leave nothing about them |
| D-116 | Documentation upkeep is a standing rule (Brandon, 2026-10-25): an Overview (`docs/overview.md`), Admin Guide (`docs/admin-guide/`), User Guide (`docs/user-guide/`) and step-by-step Testing Guide (`docs/testing-guide/`) are kept current with the newest version. A feature is not done until its Admin Guide page, User Guide page (if users touch it), Testing Guide script(s) and, if status or the module list changes, the Overview are updated in the same PR (Sections 7.4 and 9.4). Each index carries an "Applies to" line naming the main commit it was checked against | Update the guides in a periodic sweep | Guides written after the fact drift from the product; the same-PR rule keeps them true to the latest version |
| D-117 | Staff merges (household merge and duplicate contact merge) run the merge DML in system mode after BarnCRM's own permission check and a record edit-access check; no Delete on Account or Contact is granted (Brandon, 2026-10-25; Testing Guide finding U2; fix branch `fix/staff-merges-system-mode` in progress) | Grant Delete on Account and Contact to staff permission sets | Staff can merge what they may edit without gaining the power to delete any person or household |
| D-118 | Two new optional modules for both editions, depending on Core only, planned after the Core quality pass and after C-45 and C-46, not scheduled to an iteration (Brandon, 2026-10-25): **Corporate Hub** (Section 5.8, CH-01 to CH-14): document and policy library on Salesforce Files, policy acknowledgments, compliance calendar, shipped country-neutral compliance templates as custom metadata (corporate or state annual report, annual tax return, GDPR; recommended extras CH-06 to CH-08), and board governance (roster and terms, meetings, packets, minutes, quorum, motions and votes, conflict-of-interest disclosures, board giving participation with Giving optional, board dashboard complementing F-07); **Portal Builder** (Section 5.9, PB-01 to PB-05): logged-in portals from templates (Board first, then Volunteer, Service, Partner), each template needing only its own module, superseding Section 6.1.1 item 12 and pairing with C-42. Access direction set: portal users log in without Salesforce user licenses; the architecture choice (off-platform portal, recommended, or on-platform Salesforce Site) is open (Section 11.2 item 30; amended by D-120: the on-platform Salesforce Site option at no additional license cost is required, and the off-platform portal becomes an optional later connector). Board governance sits in Corporate Hub, not in the portal, as the integrator's call (reversible), so staff can run the board without a portal. No Experience Cloud dependency (D-09, ADR-0013, Section 1.5); Files count against the org's file storage; standard reports and dashboards only. Each module needs a new ADR before build | A Board Portal module holding board data; board members as licensed internal users; an Experience Cloud portal | Owner request for board and compliance tools in either edition, with portal logins that cost the customer no Salesforce licenses |
| D-119 | Multi-brand management (C-47) is a planned Core feature for both editions, built after v1.0 and not scheduled to an iteration (Brandon, 2026-10-26): brand records, a Brand lookup on records, brand-aware outputs consumed by module features, and brand filters, using standard Salesforce features only (no Experience Cloud), with the canonical model updated before any object is added (Section 5.1, Section 6.1 item 17). Whether brands ever restrict visibility was open (Section 11.2 item 31; resolved by D-120). Advanced Territory Management (C-40) stays planned after v1.0 per D-98 (made a module by D-120). **Amended 2026-10-26 (Brandon, D-120):** brands belong to Legal Entity records, brands are assigned to people, households and organizations many-to-many, and an optional brand-restricted visibility setting is added | Not planned; a brand per separate org; Experience Cloud branding only | Owner request ("Is advanced territory management and multi-brand management on the roadmap? If not, please add them now."); parent organizations with programs, chapters, affiliates or DBAs need each brand's own name, logo and sender on receipts and letters within one org |
| D-120 | Modules, brands and portals (Brandon, 2026-10-26). (1) Advanced Territory Management (C-40) becomes an optional module, **BarnCRM Territory Management**, for both editions, depending on Core only, planned after v1.0 and not scheduled; it keeps the C-40 ID, as the single-feature modules C-45 and C-46 did (amends D-98). (2) New optional module **BarnCRM LWC Builder** (Section 5.10, LB-01 to LB-06; module features get their own prefix, as CH and PB did), both editions, depending on Core only, planned after v1.0 and not scheduled: admins build components on a drag-and-drop grid canvas; the recommended design is one packaged runtime renderer reading saved definitions in user mode, with nothing deployed at run time (generating and deploying LWC source through the Metadata API is the recorded alternative, not for v1). (3) Multi-brand management (C-47) adds Legal Entity records that brands belong to (legal name, tax or registration ID, registered address, fiscal year start; gifts, funds, receipts and receipt numbering belong to the receiving entity; Corporate Hub obligations and the GDPR controller per entity; moving a gift between entities is an audited correction), many-to-many brand assignment for people, households and organizations, and an optional brand-restricted visibility setting, off by default, kept by share rows BarnCRM maintains (resolves Section 11.2 item 31; amends D-119). (4) Portal Builder must include at least one option on Salesforce with logins at no additional license cost: option (B), a Salesforce Site with BarnCRM's own login layer, is required; the off-platform option (A) becomes an optional later connector; option (C), donated internal licenses, is recorded for small boards; confirming (B) waits on a check of Salesforce's contract terms (Section 11.2 item 30; amends D-118). Each module, the brand sharing code and the portal login layer need a new ADR before build | Territory management in Core; generating and deploying LWC source through the Metadata API for v1; an "Is legal entity" flag on Brand; a single Brand lookup on people; brand separation through standard sharing rules only; an off-platform portal as the only login option | Owner direction: "Please change territory management to a module. I'd also like to add an LWC builder as a module."; "some of these may be different legal entities so please consider that for impact"; "There should be an option to restrict record visibility based on the brand(s) assigned to the record and what brand(s) the user has access to."; "There needs to be at least one option to build with Salesforce with logins but without additional cost." Standard sharing rules cannot express many-to-many brand membership |

| D-125 | Integrations and migrations (Brandon, 2026-10-26; Section 6.1.4, C-48, Section 11.2 item 32): the review's top 10 are post-1.0 roadmap items and its decisions O-1 to O-12 are open, deferred until after Core QA. Integrations wait until Core QA and development are done, preferably after v1.0 unless applicable now. The in-app Integrations page (C-48, being built now) is roadmap only ("In Progress") with a request path for users and providers. Mass Action Scheduler is never added or integrated (unmaintained). | D-134 | Account record types (RT1; Brandon, 2026-10-28, all eight recommendations approved; ADR-0141, written on branch `feature/rt1-docs`, amends ADR-0005, ADR-0041, ADR-0074 and ADR-0124; Sections 4.6, 10.3): (1) the Household record type stays packaged and active and is always listed in Setup, but no user is ever given it (amended the same day, adjustment A below): a new packaged permission set `BarnCRM_Households` carries only the Households tab and is given by the existing tab sync while `HouseholdUpkeep` is on, and the role sets lose the record type and the tab; (2) Core no longer ships an Organization record type: organizations are the accounts of one record type the org already has, stored in the setting "Record type for organizations" (`Organization_Record_Type__c`), which sits in General and is always visible; (3) detection when nothing is stored: a record type already used by accounts first, then Organization, Business Account, Business, Default, never anything named Household or a person record type, and "No record type" (Master) when the org has none; (4) one record type means organization, for now; (5) the Households tab is hidden while Households is off (ADR-0124 rule); (6) in mwdbdevsb, BarnCRM's leftover Organization record type is deactivated and deleted later, after the redesign is deployed there and a count shows no record uses it, with owner approval; (7) `barn-sales` is kept (it has DLRS) and one short-lived scratch org is approved for the verification spike; (8) a read-only check of `barn-npc` is approved. A formula field `Account.Is_Organization__c` replaces fixed Organization record type references in list views and page rules. **Amended 2026-10-28 (Brandon, adjustment A, after the verification spike):** spike V2 showed that a user whose profile defaults to no record type (Master) and who is given Household by a permission set gets Household as the effective default for every account created without a record type (API and the New button), so "No record type" organizations would become households. So the Household record type is never given to users; BarnCRM creates households only through its system-mode writer; the user-mode paths (New Household on Hub home, household merge and split) move to system mode with explicit access checks; staff create households with BarnCRM's New Household, not the standard New button. Follow-up spike: a user without the record type can read and edit an existing household (REST and UI API), cannot change another account to Household, and the standard New button defaults to no record type with no Household option. **RT5 (Brandon, 2026-10-29):** the packaged Organization record type is removed outright (only BarnCRM's test orgs ever had it), built in PR #712, landing batch 13. A user who cannot use the organization record type is told rather than given it: an Import row is rejected with the reason, sample data refuses to load, and Health Check warns; there is no dynamic grant. Detection is org-wide and ignores the running user's access. "No record type" (Master) is offered and saved only where the org has no candidate record type | Give users the Household record type through the permission set (V2 above); an admin Setup step to add an organization record type before turn-on; a trigger that resets a defaulted Household | Ship both record types inactive and activate on turn-on (an upgrade or redeploy writes inactive back); create record types on demand by a Metadata API callout (needs an admin-authorized app); admin creates them in Setup (about six steps); several organization record types | Owner feedback 2026-10-26: no record types added unless Households is on, and an existing Organization, Business or Default record type is used instead of a new Organization; a package cannot leave out or deactivate a record type it ships, so visibility through a permission set is the platform floor |

Import and Bulk Update also leave the navigation of the BarnCRM Admin and BarnCRM Admin Console apps, where they were still listed; their tabs stay so their Data Manager cards open them; the everyday apps list Data Manager where Find was, so Find and Export holders still reach it. Error Log is always the last tab of any app that includes it, module tabs added at deploy included (a static check enforces it), and the Import wizard itself is unchanged (a separate design pass on Import is coming). Delete Many Records is the last Data Manager card; its warning appears in a confirmation dialog when the Delete Many Records tile is selected (Continue opens the tool, Cancel does nothing), not as an inline warning box on the Start a Task section | Find staying in the navigation (D-51); an inline warning box on the delete card (D-133) | Owner request: the data tools live in one place, and a warning reads at the moment of the risky choice instead of always on screen |

(Brandon, 2026-10-28, all seven recommendations approved; Section 4.9; engines and the steps after step one unchanged): (1) step one of the Import wizard becomes a page of choice cards: People and Organizations; Gifts (shown only when an active template maps the `Gift` entity, so Core never references Opportunity or Giving); One Kind of Record; Records With Their Related Records; Delete the Records a File Lists; Your Templates (every active template with its description and last load date); each template shows what it creates, built from its mapping, and the copy stops saying "contacts" and "one object" only; (2) "Start a New Template From This One" copies a people or gift template's mapping, matching and row rules under a new name; (3) Core ships two more people templates, Organization List and Couples and Households, added at install and on upgrade like the others (D-55); (4) the shipped "Generic contact list" is renamed "People and Organizations List" for new installs only; an org's existing copy is left as it is; (5) Core one-object templates for Affiliations and Relationships are planned for later, after an org check of reciprocal relationship automation and affiliation primary rules under bulk load, not built now; (6) the Delete card is shown only to holders of Bulk Delete Records; (7) built as two pull requests under about 800 changed lines each: the cards, the creates summary and copy (with item 6) first, then new template from this one, the two templates and the rename. (1) A small Core component kit of `c-barn-*` LWCs: `barnPageShell`, `barnToolTile` and `barnToolGrid`, `barnEmptyState` and `barnHelp` in kit part 1; `barnStepFlow`, `barnVisualPicker` and `barnStatTile` in kit part 2; `barnSaveBar`; `barnConfirmModal` extended. SLDS 2 styling hooks only. (2) Twelve Core UX PRs in this order: 1 UX kit 1 (shell, tiles, empty state, help); 2 Admin navigation (tab lists, tab motifs, one name per tool; dropping the Bulk Update and Import tabs and adding Find needs Brandon's decision); 3 Data Manager on the kit; 4 UX kit 2 and Import (step flow, visual picker, stat tile; Import path, Title Case steps, More Options); 5 Org Optimizer (overview tiles with live badges, no nested cards, Title Case tabs, storage bars); 6 Settings console (grouped navigation with icons, descriptions to helptext, save bar, compact setup notes, defaults banner); 7 Setup Checklist and first run (Setup Assistant pattern, LightningModal first run); 8 Find and Bulk tools; 9 Duplicate Matching (shell, review badge, side-by-side pairs, paging); 10 Copy and accessibility sweep (Title Case breaches, new-tab link pattern, shorter labels); 11 Home hub; 12 Record panels. The listed order stands, but screenshots of oi-test on 2026-10-28 moved Duplicate Matching (PR 9) ahead of Find and Bulk tools (PR 8) in priority and raised Admin navigation to high impact. (3) Module screens (Rollup Builder, Giving settings, gift batch, Volunteers) come later and are recorded only. (4) **Org Optimizer** is the single user-facing name of the data-quality tool, and its data section is named **Data Quality**; "Tidy and Check Your Data", "Data Hygiene" and "Data quality" as a tool name are retired in user-facing text (the `Data_Hygiene` API name stays). PR 1 is being built on `feature/ux-kit-1` | Per-screen fixes with no shared kit; keeping several names for one tool; restyling modules in the same pass | One kit gives every Core screen the same shell, tiles and help, so later PRs only compose it; one name for the tool ends the mismatch between tab, page and docs; modules wait because Core is what they stand on (D-38) |
| D-141 | Batch testing (Brandon, 2026-10-28; Section 9.6; amends D-131): "I want the testing to be done in batches and optimized to go faster wherever prudent" and "I want this testing optimization for everything with this project going forward." (1) Feature PRs run Static checks, DCO, local Jest and the `scripts/ci` checks only, with no org gate (full or light). (2) An integrator merges every ready feature branch into one `landing/batch-N` branch (precedent: PR #219, "Landing batch 7"), resolves conflicts once, refreshes `docs/product-plan.md` from BMemory once and writes the root docs. (3) The integrator runs the full org gates on `barn-sales` and `oi-test` in parallel on the landing head (each org has its own lock). (4) Failures are fixed on the landing branch; the landing PR is squash-merged only after both gates pass, and the included feature PRs are closed as landed. (5) Full gates still run on every batch, and the gate scripts are tuned for speed only where no coverage that catches real failures is lost. **Amended 2026-10-28:** Brandon approved all six speedup proposals of PR #664 ("Yes to all"). What each became: (1) Core's three config stages deploy in one request and (2) every module deploys in one request, each falling back to separate stages on the opaque failure (no component error); (3) a package unchanged since the last successful deploy to the same org is not deployed again (one record per org id outside the repository; any doubt, including a newer deploy in the org, means a full deploy; `--full` forces one; Apex still deploys in release runs, because the coverage check relies on the recompile); (4) shortening the Apex test run is separate work; (5) and (6) were superseded the same day by D-142: `--light` is retired but `--relevant` is kept as the incremental default, and the ruleset keeps one required "Org tests (local)" status instead of one per org. Item (5) of this row and the parallel full gates on every batch are amended by D-142 | An org gate (full or light) per feature PR; running the two org gates one after the other | One deploy and test run per org per batch, both orgs at once; every Apex test still runs on every batch before anything reaches `main` |
| D-142 | Incremental and release gates (Brandon, 2026-10-28; Section 9.6; amends D-141; supersedes proposals 5 and 6 of the PR #664 speedups as approved earlier the same day): "I'd mostly like these initial tests to be as fast as possible with an end-to-end test of the entire new version once a significant new version's initial development is done and integrated. I don't want as much testing for all the incremental changes." (1) Incremental gate, the default for every landing batch (`run-org-tests.sh <alias>`, no flag): one org, `barn-sales` (the faster gate, about 18 minutes full against about 24 on `oi-test`; the Sales Cloud contacts shape most installs have; DLRS present); the staged deploy with the D-141 speedups (combined config and module stages, unchanged packages skipped, Apex included); then the Apex changed since `origin/main` plus the `@IsTest(critical=true)` classes deploy again with RunRelevantTests; no coverage floor; it posts the required "Org tests (local)" status with a description starting "Incremental:", so it can merge a batch. Jest runs in the required Static checks job on the same head, not in the gate. Accepted risk: the relevant-test selection can miss indirect paths and non-Apex changes (objects, permission sets, flows, custom metadata) that an unselected test depends on; the release run catches them before a version ships. (2) Release gate, once a significant version's development is done and integrated (`run-org-tests.sh <alias> --release`, an explicit flag so nobody runs it for an increment): every package deployed and every test class run, with the coverage floor, on `barn-sales` and `oi-test` in parallel; then the version is re-validated and deployed to `barn-npc` and `mwdbdevsb` (each `mwdbdevsb` deploy needs Brandon's approval), then QA and UX tested. (3) `--light` is retired, redundant with the incremental gate. (4) The `main` ruleset keeps the single "Org tests (local)" context; no per-org required statuses. The "Held" guard stays, because the release gates post from two orgs in parallel | Full gates on both orgs for every batch (D-141); per-org required status contexts (PR #664 proposal 6); retiring `--relevant` (PR #664 proposal 5) | Increments get a fast check of what changed; each version gets every test and the coverage floor on both org shapes before it ships |
| D-143 | Automation performance (Brandon, 2026-10-28; Section 6.0 item 10): "Yes please speed up automation for testing and performance. Feel free to consolidate/refactor as long as no functionality is lost." The product's own trigger chain (households, addresses, rollups, greetings, matching, gifts) is profiled in `oi-test` and made faster in small PRs (`perf/automation-N`, each under about 800 lines) that keep behavior: same records, same field values, same async jobs. Measured first, ranked by saving and risk; the integrator runs the gates in the landing batch (D-141, D-142). |
| D-144 | Admin navigation and tab icons, UX PR 2 (Brandon, 2026-10-28; settles the open point in D-140 item 2). (1) Find, Import and Bulk Update stay off every app's navigation and open from their Data Manager cards (D-135); the Bulk Update card says bulk updates start in Find and opens Find. (2) Error Log is always an app's last tab. (3) The everyday apps list records first and Data Manager last among Core tabs (BarnCRM Console keeps Addresses, Relationships, Affiliations and Household Members before Data Manager for its subtab mappings), so module tabs follow Data Manager in both; the two admin apps hold the same tabs. (4) Each Core tab gets its own motif whose number matches the icon Salesforce documents (the name after the colon is only a note): Settings Custom67 Gears, Data Manager Custom57 BuildingBlock, Org Optimizer Custom94 Stethoscope, Duplicate Matching Custom90 Scales, Error Log Custom53 Bell, Find Custom39 Telescope, Import Custom18 Form, Households Custom15 People, Organizations Custom24 Building, Addresses Custom78 Map, Relationships Custom37 Bridge, Affiliations Custom33 Desk, Household Members Custom14 Hands; Home (Custom1 Heart) and Bulk Update (Custom19 Wrench) unchanged; a static test keeps them valid and unique. (5) Data Manager, Org Optimizer, Duplicate Matching, Find and Import use `barnPageShell` with `title-hidden`, so the App Page header prints the name once; Settings follows in UX PR 6. (6) "Org Optimizer" is the one tool name with "Data Quality" its section; renaming screens is UX PR 5. Recorded for later: module tab motifs carry the same guessed names (for example five Giving tabs on Custom25, the alarm clock) | Adding Find as a tab; keeping guessed motif names; leaving Data Manager mid-menu in the console app | One icon per page that matches its header and console tab; one order across the four apps; the page name printed once |

Core's post-install steps rewrite an old stored value once, as bookkeeping with no Recent Changes row; until then `chosenSuite` and Recent Changes read the old values as the new ones, because the org test gate deploys without the post-install steps and logged rows keep the old codes. API names (`Suite__c`, `Core_SuiteChoice_*`, `setupStepSuite`) stay. ADR-0143 (`0143-edition-internal-values.md`), built in PR #716, landing batch 13 | Keeping the old values (a permanent translation between two names, and a real customer migration once a package version exists); migrating with no reading of old values (test orgs deployed by the org gate would lose their edition until Finish Setup ran); rewriting logged Recent Changes rows (an audit trail) | No customer has installed BarnCRM and no package version exists, so this is the cheapest moment; one name per edition in Setup and in code |

| D-153 | Staging retention and undo (Brandon, 2026-10-30, "Undo should be able to bring the deleted record(s) back from the trash, not create a redundant copy"; Section 11.2 item 37; C-49b): the retention options (delete once complete, after 30 days as default, after 90 days, never) apply to staging rows; undo never depends on staging rows and never re-inserts a copy; a record an undo restores is undeleted from the Recycle Bin (as bulk delete undo already does), and one no longer there is reported as not restorable, never recreated. ## 13. Glossary (nonprofit and platform terms, for agents and contributors)

- **Appeal:** a fundraising effort (mailing, event, campaign). We avoid the word "campaign" in the UI because Salesforce's Campaign object means something specific and is unavailable on Platform licenses.
- **Commitment:** a promise to give, either a pledge (fixed total) or a recurring gift (open-ended).
- **Fund:** a designation or restriction on how money may be used (NPSP: General Accounting Unit; NPC: Gift Designation).
- **Hard credit:** the donor legally credited with a gift. **Soft credit:** recognition for influencing a gift without giving it (spouse, solicitor, honoree, matching employer relationship).
- **Household:** the unit of relationship and recognition for individual donors; one mailing, one greeting, one giving history.
- **LYBUNT / SYBUNT:** donors who gave Last Year But Unfortunately Not This year, and Some Year But Unfortunately Not This year; standard lapsed-donor lists.
- **Receipt:** the document a donor uses for tax purposes; in the US it must state the amount, date, organization, and whether goods or services were provided.
- **Restricted fund:** money a donor or funder limited to a purpose; the balance is a compliance obligation.
- **Stewardship plan:** a scheduled sequence of touches after a gift or milestone (NPSP: Engagement Plan).
- **Tribute:** a gift made in honor or in memory of someone.
- **2GP:** second-generation managed packaging. **PBO:** Partner Business Org. **Dev Hub:** the org that owns package definitions. **Push upgrade:** the publisher-initiated upgrade of installed orgs.
- **Org shape:** the license and package configuration of an org (Platform-only, Sales Cloud, NPSP, Agentforce Nonprofit) used in CI.

---

*End of document. Everything above is intended to be enough to begin. When in doubt, return to Section 1.3 and ask which principle the decision serves.*
